# Implementation index and phase map

**Status:** MAP — identifier families and landing inventory. Not a design SoT.
Last verification: 2026-08-28, `dev` = `fabc2b7c0` (docs-flow repair on current origin/dev).

**Purpose.** One page that answers "what does this identifier mean, which doc
owns it, and what has actually landed on `dev`?" The wallet/staking work spans
several plan documents whose numbering schemes evolved independently; this
index is the disambiguation layer. It is a *map*, not a status-of-record —
each track's own plan doc remains authoritative for design detail, and where
a plan doc's status table disagrees with landed code, **code wins** (§6 lists
the known disagreements at the verification date rather than editing those
docs from here).

**Method note (2026-07-18) — gate rows decay at the referenced item's rate.** A gate row
("blocked on X", "X is unbuilt") is a claim about *another* item's state and goes stale
when that item moves, not when its host document does. The **cleared-gate sweep is a
landing obligation of the PR that clears the gate**, not of the docs that name it: a PR
that builds or lands X must grep for rows naming X as a blocker (docs *and* code
comments) and correct them in the same landing. Blocked-when-actually-unblocked is the
expensive direction — nobody re-checks a gate that says it is closed. (Instances swept
2026-07-18: the M1 `K_COVER` gate list, the SP-R0 "SP-6 unbuilt" rows in FOLLOWUPS and
the 2D2 plan §2/§12, the WI-1 "made the chain live" comment — PR #333 and the SP-R0
Round-0 exit.)

**Verification stamp.** Statuses below were verified against landed code.
The unified stamp is `dev` = `fabc2b7c0` as of 2026-08-28 (§5). Older
code-anchored rows keep the SHA they were verified at in the cell. When
you update a status here, re-verify against code (`git grep`, not plan
prose) and move this stamp.

**Maintenance discipline** (institutionalized as
[`.cursor/rules/94-tracking-index.mdc`](../../.cursor/rules/94-tracking-index.mdc) —
new identifier families register here at birth; Phase 3+ / Stage 3+ work
items start with an index row).

- **Row format:** every status cell *leads* with the current status and its
  as-of date (e.g. `Landed (as of 2026-08-01)`); dated `UPDATE YYYY-MM-DD:`
  entries append *below/after* that line as the history chain. Readers get
  the answer without parsing the chain.
- **Two verification classes:** *code-anchored* items cite a `git grep`
  target on `dev`; *decision-anchored* items (round closures, wire freezes,
  contract docs — no code artifact) cite the commit hash or PR number of the
  doc edit that closed them.
- **Section contract:** rule 94 refers to §2 (identifier-family registry),
  §4–§5 (live / built-unwired / missing inventory), and §6
  (known-stale-statuses ledger) by role. Their meanings — and this numbering
  — are load-bearing; a restructuring that moves them updates
  `.cursor/rules/94-tracking-index.mdc` in the same PR.
- This doc never carries design content — link to the owning doc instead.
- New identifier families (a new prefix series in any plan doc) get a §2 row
  in the same PR that introduces them, after the rule-94 prefix-uniqueness
  check.

---

## 1. The collision this index untangles

Two independent schemes both used the bare tokens `2a / 2b / 2c / 2d`, and
one token (`2c-2b`) was reused *within* a single scheme:

| Token | Engine-layer meaning (`WALLET_REWRITE_PLAN.md`) | Bond-construction meaning (`ARCHIVAL_BOND_PR2_CHAIN.md`) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2a | Phase 2a — send path | Bond-PR 2a — synthetic-tree round-trip KAT (#156) |
| 2b | Phase 2b — stake lifecycle | Bond-PR 2b — StakeEngine actor, seed-owning model (#157) |
| 2c | Phase 2c — addresses/proofs | Bond-PR 2c-1 / 2c-2a / 2c-2b — real-tree KAT, Model D wiring, request path |
| 2d | Phase 2d — cold bundles | Bond-PR 2d-1 / 2d-2 — `P`-scan layer, `P`-isolated transport |

**Resolution (pinned 2026-06-19, `ARCHIVAL_BOND_PR2_CHAIN.md` §0):** the bond
chain is prefixed **`Bond-PR`** in doc prose; bare **`Phase 2x`** always means
the engine layer. Branch names are immutable history and keep their original
(unprefixed) names.

**The `2c-2b` double-use.** Within the bond chain itself, `2c-2b` names two
different deliverables in sequence:

1. **Bond-PR 2c-2b (request path)** — JoinMarket bond request layer, landed
   `feat/archival-bond-request` #163, plan doc
   [`ARCHIVAL_BOND_REQUEST_2C2B_PLAN.md`](ARCHIVAL_BOND_REQUEST_2C2B_PLAN.md).
2. **2c-2b re-scope (GF-7 scheduler seam)** — block-timed entry-seam planning
   plus GF-7 measurement hooks, landed PR #255 (2026-07-05), plan doc
   [`ARCHIVAL_BOND_2C_GF7_HOOKS.md`](ARCHIVAL_BOND_2C_GF7_HOOKS.md).

When you see `2c-2b` in prose, the linked plan doc disambiguates; new prose
should say "request path" or "GF-7 seam" explicitly.

| **OA-1** | The wallet's **operator alarm channel** — the snapshot a human reads when a guarantee the wallet cannot restore by itself has been lost. The `shekyl-operator-alarm` crate publishes a `watch<AlarmBoard>`: one row per watched `AlarmCondition`, each carrying its `Arming` (a check that is **not running** reads *disarmed*, never healthy — the requirement that forces a snapshot rather than an event stream) and at most one **live** `RaisedAlarm` with a stable `IncidentId`. Resolved latches leave the condition row for `AlarmBoard::unacknowledged`, a separate queue: several faults share one `AlarmCondition` while only one latches, so a per-condition slot would have to choose between the fault the operator has not seen and the unrelated one after it. `AlarmLifetime` splits alarms by whether the **observable** outlives the condition: an `Episode` leaves the board when its condition clears, a `LatchedRederived` one is held for acknowledgment because the evidence disappears while the damage stands. Its `tor_posture` module is the first producer | `ARCHIVAL_BOND_2D2_SP_T0_TOR.md` §3c (`Degraded` is "the operator-alarm hook (`82`)"; `Degraded u Ready{recovering}` renders as one incident); implementation in `rust/shekyl-operator-alarm/`, re-exported from `shekyl-engine-core`'s root | Registered 2026-08-14 at birth (rule 94 §1); prefix `OA-` passes the uniqueness check against Phase/Stage/M/Bond-PR/SP/SP-T/PR-A/PR-B/PR-E/CW/CT/GF/S/R/F/DQ/SCE/WI/M1/CB/TJ/VG/SH/LV/DS/MS/MSW/PF/WS/Q/RP/X. Not folded into `SH-` because the channel is not about serving: its producer is the tor supervisor, it is testable without a persona, and SH-2b-2 is one of its consumers rather than its owner. It is a **leaf crate** rather than an engine module because it names no engine type — an embedder should be able to render an alarm without linking the orchestrator (rule 82) | OA-1 (channel + tor-posture producer) lands in the OA-1 PR. Serving-condition variants (`Stale` / `RefreshFailing` / `PinsDropped`) land **with their emitters** in SH-2b-2, not ahead of them; `PinsDropped` is the known candidate that is neither existing lifetime class and needs durable acknowledgment first |

| **PC-D1…PC-Dn** | The **per-challenge pass record**: the serve-credit record becomes one per challenge rather than one per pair-epoch, so consensus counts passes by enumeration | [`ARCHIVAL_PER_CHALLENGE_RECORD.md`](ARCHIVAL_PER_CHALLENGE_RECORD.md) | Registered 2026-08-24 at birth (rule 94 §1); prefix `PC-` unique against CB/CR/CT/CW/DS/GF/LV/MR/MS/MSW/OA/PF/PR/RF/RP/SA/SH/SO/SP/TJ/VG/WI/WP/WS | **RULED + IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-24** (grounded `dev@863a9bf4f`); rules 26 + **07** (an admission path changed in place on a genesis-frozen surface). **`PC-D1`: no tally field on the wire, ever.** 2-of-3 needs a pass count and the reflex is a counter on the record — but whoever writes the record chooses the number (write 3, settle Served, with one read or none), which is `RF-D8`'s criterion verbatim and the same defect as a supplied `R_k` or leaf index. **The count was never supposed to be on the record**: three challenges are three events in three blocks, each already nonce-bound to its own block, so consensus counts by **enumerating the epoch's blocks**. Scope stated so it is not over-applied — this governs **transported** values only, not a node-computed count in its own LMDB; `SO-D2`'s settlement row keeps `passes`/`issued` for the reason `RF-D8` **endorsed**, and this round makes that row *more* correct. **THE HAZARD, stated first because a careful reader walks past it:** `RF-D5` already made the nonce block-bound while the opening is not, so three records under the old derivation carry three genuinely distinct countersignatures over three **identical openings** — *an artifact that looks like three tests and contains one*, satisfied by a prover that fetched one leaf once, with every signature verifying. **A reviewer checking "do these three records differ?" sees yes and stops.** **`PC-D2` — the block is IMPLICIT and the wire does not move.** The miner that closes the block issues the challenge (no thundering herd to arbitrate), so the record rides its own producer's block: nothing is carried, there is no claimed block for admission to validate, and **no path where a record names a block it did not come from** — unrepresentable, not checked. The broadcast alternative (carry the block, verify the claim) is more wire, more surface, and a new forgery axis for flexibility the mechanism does not need. **`PC-D3`:** `challenge_leaf_index` takes **`block_hash(h−1)`, the hash not the height** — a height is a reference a reorg can silently repoint, a hash names the block, and the nonce already binds that same term, so the record keeps **one** block reference instead of two that could diverge; separator → `-v2` (rule 30, one label never names two functions). **DELETION-BOUND as of 2026-08-26, and the round record says so rather than leaving it as an achievement:** `RF-D8` (i) was retracted, so `challenge_leaf_index` is back on `ARCHIVAL_CREDIT_WIRE.md` §2's deletion surface and `PC-D3` hardened a function the cutover removes. It is not wasted and must not be reverted early — it is `TJ-1`'s prescribed interim mitigation, load-bearing against the precomputable-schedule free-ride for exactly as long as the beacon still issues. The `-v2` and retired `-v1` domain-registry rows retire with it, moving `MECH1` 43 → 41 and `TEST_ONLY_EXPECTED` 8 → 7 in the same change. **`PC-D4`:** the ledger key widens `(P, s, E)` → `(P, s, E, block)`, i.e. **one record per producer per pair-epoch**, so a duplicate is the same key, refused by structure. **CORRECTED AT IMPLEMENTATION:** the old-vin dedup does **not** become that enforcer yet — widening the key makes its exact-get *vacuous* (it probes a block not yet in the DB), so it stays **pair-epoch-wide** (`archival_serve_credit_pass_count > 0`), byte-identical to pre-widening consensus. Also split out **`ArchivalPairEpochKey`** (48 B): the slash-applied trio shared the key type by coincidence of shape and is per-pair-epoch by design, as is the within-block SCE-1 pass (the block is common-mode inside one block). **`PC-D5`:** `passes` is counted by walking admitted records. **`PC-D6` — the round's largest surface and highest risk:** the widened key changes a table with FOUR readers, and **the emission gather cannot be left to infer** — it pushes one `credit_pairs` entry per row (`gather_archival_emission_epoch_snapshot`, no value gate), so three keys where it expects one **triples `credit_pairs` and inflates `r_market` silently, with no error and no test failing** unless a test asserts the fold. The gather therefore folds rows → one credit per pair-epoch **explicitly**, same shape as `SO-D1`'s enumerate-and-write. **This is `CR-D2`'s four-reader problem arriving through the KEY rather than the VALUE.** Payment stays per-pair-epoch, now preserved *by the fold* rather than by the key, so the fold is load-bearing and gets a test that fails when it is removed. **LANDED, and the removal test proved the site silent: deleting the fold turns EXACTLY ONE test in the suite red** — the one written for it; the pre-existing emission tests assert on `credit_pairs.size()` and still miss it, because they seed one row per pair-epoch and never build the state the fold acts on. **`PC-D7` — HALF collapsed; CORRECTED 2026-08-24 at implementation.** "No claimed block means nothing to verify" retires *verifying a claimed reference* and does **not** reach the check's second job — *verifying this block's assignment names this pair* — which is the **count bound** and the anti-adaptive-selection check. Discharging both would leave the pass count bounded by nothing (a pair files in every block of the epoch and inflates its own count — `TJ R1`'s free-rider margin). Held by keeping the dedup pair-epoch-wide; the surviving half is deferred on a **named blocker**: `assign_epoch` has no FFI export and no consensus caller, and the live beacon issues one challenge per pair-epoch, so the three-row state is **unreachable by construction**. Reopen when the assignment cutover lands. The `SO-D8` blocker is discharged **for the schema only** — the three-record *semantics* still waits on the urn. Both this bound and the interim sampled-leaf weakening (beacon window + per-block index ⇒ best-of-window) are written into the cutover's own inputs at `ARCHIVAL_CHALLENGE_MECHANISM.md` §9.5.1. **What does NOT change, the round's main saving:** the vin layout, `ArchivalServeCreditResponse`'s fields, `canonical_bytes` length, the pruned half, `SERVE_CREDIT_PRUNED_MAX_BYTES` and its twin — and since no persisted block byte moves, **rule 42 does NOT fire — no persisted-block version bump; the **LMDB schema version DID bump to V10** (separate guard, separate question: a V9 datadir's 48-byte serve-credit rows would be silently missed by point reads and then FATAL on scans — delete-and-resync)** (recorded so nobody adds one "to be safe": a bump with no wire change is a false signal costing the next reader a grounding pass). **Cost: three openings instead of one, already priced** — `ARCHIVAL_SETTLEMENT_WRITER.md` §2.1 computed `k = 3·D/SEB`, one record per challenge, so its figures stand and the per-epoch reading would have been *cheaper than budgeted* because it did a third of the testing. **§5.2 consequence for `SO-D2`:** its settlement key was ruled byte-identical to `ArchivalServeCreditKey` so one key probes both tables; that key now widens to 56 B while the settlement table stays per-pair-epoch, so the shared rationale **expires** (retired justification, not a defect — the tables answer at different granularities) and the landed #554 accessors need their own 48-byte key type — **now provided as `ArchivalPairEpochKey`**; whichever of this branch and #554 merges second owns the follow-through, and the two **will conflict in `db_lmdb.cpp`**. **Both landed 2026-08-26** (#554, then #556 as `05963f375`); the conflict materialised as predicted, was resolved in #556, and the follow-through is complete — `ArchivalPairEpochKey` is the settlement/slash key and no settlement accessor references the widened one. Of `PC-D1`…`PC-D7`, only `PC-D3` is deletion-bound (above); `PC-D2`/`PC-D4`/`PC-D6`/`PC-D7` concern record multiplicity and settlement, not the record's contents, and survive the pure-attestation payload unchanged |
| **RF-D1…RF-Dn** | Serve-credit **response format** design-round dispositions: the genesis-frozen response wire | [`ARCHIVAL_RESPONSE_FORMAT.md`](ARCHIVAL_RESPONSE_FORMAT.md) | Registered 2026-08-18 at birth (rule 94 §1); prefix `RF-` checked unique against CB/CR/CT/CW/DS/GF/LV/MR/MS/MSW/OA/PF/PR/RP/SA/SH/SP/TJ/VG/WI/WS | **Round CLOSED 2026-08-21 — all of `RF-D1`…`RF-D10` ruled and implemented on `dev` (PR #522). Head corrected 2026-08-23: this row read "Round OPEN" while its own tail already recorded both artifacts landed, so the row contradicted itself and the leading status is the half a reader trusts.** Opened 2026-08-18, grounded `dev@69e76a7be`; unblocked by the carrier round's ruling, **RULED 2026-08-18 and merged to `dev` in PR #501**. **RF-D3 RESOLVED 2026-08-19 and the round is now PURE TRANSCRIPTION — every input pinned.** `r` still deletes, but this round did not settle it: the 2026-08-10 ruling did, and the round had **restated its argument incorrectly**. The ruling's argument is *shared chooser plus an existence bound* — `r` and `cb_out_key` are chosen by the same party, so `r` never provided pre-signing resistance ("a second producer-chosen random term does not strengthen a property that fails exactly when the producer defects"), and `block_hash(h−1)` substitutes because it "cannot exist before block h−1 does, regardless of any party's behaviour". The round had converted *not choosable* into *ungrindable* and written a falsifier for the paraphrase; that falsifier is pre-answered by the ruling's own honest-strength sentence (producer-of-both gets exactly one block of lead — the conceded bound, not a break). **Third dissolve-on-grounding in this arc** after the W₂ floor check and the Pi-4 device-requirement question, same class each time: a residual carried into a new round without re-grounding its reasoning. Sharpening added *by this round and attributed as such*: grinding the nonce has **no objective** — it is a binding input, not a threshold, so no value is better for an adversary; whatever grind-value exists lives on the **assignment** side, priced at discarded-block cost. Residuals kept visible: fully-collusive passes remain possible, priced by the 2-of-3 quadratic and the outer window. **IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-19 with `RF-D5`** (the doc leads the PR; the code landed with it, and the code found a decision the doc had not reached). `r` is **replaced**, not deleted — same arity, new source — so `attestation_nonce` takes `prev_block_hash`, the witness blob goes `r(32)+count(8)+sigs` → `count(8)+sigs`, and the cap moves 866,600 → **866,568** in three places with three enforcement mechanisms (C++ runtime FFI gate, the retention authority, and `shekyl-levin`'s const-asserted duplicate). **`RF-D5`:** the deletion is not a removal — it requires `block_hash(h−1)` to **arrive**, so the `#[repr(C)]` ctx widens and C++ populates it. All-zeros is refused with its own code (`ERR_PREVHASH_UNPOPULATED`); **no readability flag**, because a verifier holding a block has parsed its header so the arm could never fire, and a flag is itself caller-populated so it would be forgotten alongside the hash. **Tested:** five C++ call sites forgot the field and were refused loudly at build time — under the flag design all five would have passed silently. **Rule 30 satisfied by construction:** preserving arity let the frozen cross-language KAT re-anchor with nonce, countersignature and root byte-identical — a regenerated vector would have been checkable only against the code it tests. **Rule 42 checked and has NO instance here** (globs, the CI gate's scope, and the purpose all exclude a consensus transport blob whose integrity is the mined root); no bump owed, recorded so nobody adds one. **Ordering trace:** `verify_block_attestation` is a pure predicate, so a losing alt block leaves no residue — the unvalidated `prev_id` on the alt path is an ordering smell, not a soundness hole; the constraint *validated predecessor, not `prev_id` as supplied* is stated on the field regardless, and the alt-path reorder is filed separately as independent hardening. Green: C++ 1014/0/7 across 152 suites; Rust 257+8+188+66. Inputs: (1) leaf chunk **pruned-side by construction** (CR-D2); (2) **reserved padding field, no scheme** — TJ-H RULED 2026-08-08, mitigation at the Tor layer; (3) **nonce anchor = `cb_out_key` of block `h`** — ruled 2026-08-10 with fork 2 closed; (4) **`r` deleted**, nonce `H(block_hash(h−1) ‖ cb_out_key ‖ P ‖ s ‖ E)`; (5) **CR-F2's `prefix_hash`/tx-id change lands here** — carried as a first-class input rather than a cross-reference, because a fact living only in the round that discovered it is one the round that needs it will not read. **RF-D1/D2/D4 DRAFTED 2026-08-20 (§3.5) — and it is TWO ARTIFACTS.** *(This clause read "implementation pending" until 2026-08-24; both artifacts landed 2026-08-20 and the row said so 900 words later. Corrected when a review finding about this row's **head** made it obvious the **tail** had the same defect twice — fixing a status line and leaving the row that repeats it is the exact failure this row now documents.)* Premise corrected before drafting: TJ-H's reserved padding belongs to the **Tor-served shard payload** (its attack is an observed response size — *"every padded byte crosses two Tor legs"*), not the on-chain vin, so `RF-D1`/`RF-D2` and `RF-D4` govern different wires and cannot share a boundary; §9.5's live-remainder list separates them. One slice regardless, on a shared **validation surface**: does each artifact make its hashed region unambiguous to a verifier? **On-chain:** kept/pruned boundary is structural (the vin/prunable split — a field's side is where it is written, not a convention a pruner infers), with the **pairing stated not inferred** entries in serve-credit-vin order, one per serve-credit vin, mirroring `pass_signatures[i]` ↔ i-th pass header — and **stating the pairing is what removes the count field**, which would have been a `MUST`-equal check on a length the tx already determines). **RF-D2:** `hybrid_signature` → `ed25519_countersignature: [u8; 64]`, a **fixed array** — the name no longer asserts contents it lacks (the `WITNESS_PREFIX_LEN` failure *in advance*), and the length prefix plus `> PQC_HYBRID_SINGLE_SIG_LEN` bound check both vanish because a wrong length is now unrepresentable; `CR-F2`'s tx-id change is paid for the split anyway, so paying it and keeping the name would be spending the change and keeping the defect. **RF-D6 (ruled in the same draft):** `segment_subroot_rk` and `leaf_index_in_segment` come OFF the wire — **soundness, not thrift**. `R_k` is read locally (`LeafStore::frozen_segment`) and the leaf index is derived (`challenge_leaf_index`), so a verifier trusting either wire value lets the prover pick its own tree root or its own leaf, defeating the challenge. Kept side drops 266 → **230 B ≈ 5.9 GB/yr**. **RF-D4 (served payload):** `leaf_count varint ‖ padding_len varint ‖ segment_bytes ‖ padding_bytes`, only `segment_bytes` hashed against `R_k`; a leaf **count** rather than a byte length makes multiple-of-128 structural, and an explicit `padding_len` keeps the frame self-delimiting instead of deriving padding extent from `content-length`. **RF-D7:** "read-anything" is about **content, never length** — an earlier cut claimed the response was "bounded by the transport cap", an unchecked premise (`shekyl-p-serve` has request-head/concurrency/drain caps but **no response-length cap**; the fetcher's `MAX_RESPONSE_BODY_SIZE` is 256 MB = 77× a segment). `padding_len` is adversary-declared, so readers MUST reject `padding_len > leaf_count × LEAF_BYTES` **before allocating or draining** — bounding a body at 2× a segment, derived from the payload rather than picked, with a rule-21 reopen for any scheme wanting >100% overhead (which argues against TJ-H's own "every padded byte crosses two Tor legs" cost leg). Enforceable **only because RF-D4 made `padding_len` explicit**: with extent derived from `content-length` the check would arrive after the bytes did. `content-length` cannot stand in — it does not locate the split, self-authentication is by **reconstruction** (`recompute_segment_r_k` hashes what was received, so any trailing byte breaks it outright), and it is a property of the transport rather than the frozen format. Posture ruled **write-zero, read-anything** — strictness on the write side where relaxing is free, permissiveness on the read side where tightening later would be the breaking change — so a future scheme needs **no flag day** (rule 75). **Pinned now because there is no counterparty:** outside `shekyl-curve-tree`'s own store ops, `recompute_segment_r_k`'s only caller is `p-serve/tests/store_axis.rs:105`, a test — there is **no production fetcher**, so this is the definition rather than a change to a shared contract, with one side to change instead of two. **ARTIFACT B IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-20** — `shekyl_curve_tree::served_frame::ServedFrameHeader`, the served payload only; `RF-D1`/`RF-D2`/`RF-D6` (the on-chain record) were **at that moment** still to land, as a **separate validation surface** (rule 19: different crates, different tests, a C++ serializer artifact B never touches — the shared *design* question did not make them a shared *validation* question). Placement decided by which crate a **reader** must already have: a fetcher cannot verify without `recompute_segment_r_k`, so `shekyl-curve-tree` is in its graph by necessity and every other home adds an edge or declares a second `LEAF_BYTES` (which was promoted out of `redb_backend.rs`'s private `128` into `segment.rs`, derived as `SCALARS_PER_LEAF · 32`). **Write-zero is by construction:** `for_segment` takes no padding argument at all, so a future scheme's PR *adding* the parameter is the rule-21 reopening criterion rather than a pre-provisioned flexibility. **`RF-D7`'s bound is unforgettable:** both checks run inside `ServedFrameHeader::read` before it returns, so the only way to obtain the lengths is to obtain them validated — leaf-count first, which also keeps `leaf_count × LEAF_BYTES` from overflowing (ordering dependency stated at the site). **The streaming property got its forward half:** "decidable without a store read" is now written as a constraint on `padding_len` itself rather than a property of today's implementation — same move as `RF-D5`'s validated-predecessor constraint, an invariant held by circumstance having no name and no test. **Self-referential-validation problem named and partly answered:** with no counterparty, round-tripping proves only internal consistency, so the frame carries a **hand-derived** vector (`88 CB 01 00`, computed from the grammar not the encoder) and the doc now **pins the varint encoding by name** (`shekyl_curve_io`, canonical LEB128) because the first fetcher is written from the grammar and "varint" alone does not determine bytes; `store_axis.rs` was rewritten to read the frame as a fetcher will. **Consequence:** an unframeable body is now unservable (`ShardBody::flat` → `None` → shared 404), which is what buys multiple-of-`LEAF_BYTES` as structural rather than prose. Claim kept narrow: this **avoids introducing** an unbounded-padding drain, it does not close a landed one — `MAX_DRAIN_BYTES` bounds a peer's unread **request** bytes in `close_gracefully`, a different path. **A-scoped finding recorded in §3.5 rather than left to be rediscovered:** `serialize_ctsig_prunable(ba, type, count_spend_inputs(vin), vout.size())` is passed the **spend**-input count, and a serve-credit vin is explicitly not a spend input (`blockchain.cpp:3630`), so "one entry per serve-credit vin, no count field" needs a **signature widening** and every call site widens with it, including the #509 tripwire's `prunable_reserialized_size`. The tripwire itself asserts the *equivalence property* (tail length == re-serialized length), so a structure added inside `serialize_ctsig_prunable` passes — and its failure message already directs new prunable bytes exactly where `RF-D1` puts them. Gates: fmt clean, `cargo +1.94.0 clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` **0** (first run caught `manual_is_multiple_of` — a clippy-only lint `cargo check` does not have), rustdoc `-D warnings` on the persona-serving list clean. **ARTIFACT A LANDED 2026-08-20 (same PR, by ruling — the kept/pruned boundary is only reviewable with both halves visible).** **RF-D8** (CR-D2 corrected, not overridden: the leaf chunk was priced against the served payload and carried into the on-chain record; the verifier reads it from its own LMDB at `blockchain.cpp:5428`; one criterion stated once — *a value the verifier derives locally must not be transported, because transporting it lets the prover choose it* — and it took **four** fields: `segment_subroot_rk`, `leaf_index_in_segment`, the leaf chunk, and `leaf_bytes` itself, since the verifier must read the whole chunk to verify at all; the old membership check never bound the leaf to the index and a latent fixture error proved it). The **preimage keeps every term** (verifier-supplied parameters, not caller-populated fields — RF-D5's precedent), so a prover that lies about any derived value cannot produce a verifying signature: RF-D6/D8 is soundness, the ~0.9 GB/yr was the least of it. **Ruling (i)** kept the opening as supplementary consensus-checkable evidence — the one element that survives total witness collusion; `path.rs`'s "superseded" annotation was the stale text, rewritten. **RF-D9**: the serve-credit tx had never round-tripped in C++ — two independently fatal gates (write: `pqc_auths.size() == vin.size()` vs consensus-empty; parse: `bulletproofs_plus.size() == 1`), both typed off the vin now; **four sightings of one form in one change** (C++ write, C++ parse, Rust read, Rust validate — *a shape identified by absence becomes unidentifiable when the shape acquires content*, and the failures are **misattributed**: the fourth surfaced as *"needs >= 2 outputs"*), a fifth in a fixture where the **compiler's silence was the false signal**. **RF-D10** (from *why C++ and not Rust?*): the C++ vin is an **opaque `canonical_bytes` blob** on the emission-vin pattern, the pruned half an opaque per-vin record inside `serialize_ctsig_prunable` (each length-prefixed — a transport delimiter, not the count RF-D1 refused), `(P, shard, E)` through one extract FFI, the index through `shekyl_archival_challenge_leaf_index`, admission through `verify(kept, pruned, ctx)`; this also deleted the `ServeCredit`/`ArchivalServeCreditResponse` two-codec duplication; pruned weight deterministic at **5,107 B/record** for a frozen segment (relies on admission; the CT-2 test tree is 3,440 and a first pin borrowed it wrongly); the equivalence **mirror leg re-synced** (path-bound steps → FFI parse, wire round-trip/tag retired with the typed vin, index derived; B-17 → zero-geometry, B-19..21 retired, B-00/B-00b added). Kept side **≈ 99 B**; record ≈ 5,204 B. Review round on #522: four findings (guard covering one of two refusals; spike comparing to raw fixture length; `serialize_base` duplicating the pqc_auths encoding — producible but unstorable; `clear()` on the saving path normalising instead of refusing), each fixed by deleting the parameter/duplicate rather than mirroring. Rule 93: three RCT-named symbols renamed (one emitted a tag already named `ctsig_prunable`); the ~290-site remainder filed in FOLLOWUPS |
| **SO-D1…SO-Dn** | The **settlement writer**: the settlement-outcome LMDB table and the writer that fills it — inside the slash scheduler's per-epoch pass (`SO-D7`), NOT at a separate epoch-close event — the artifact that materialises `settle_epoch(passes, issued)` results for the slash fold that reads them — **not** for the emission gather, which this row's own §5 clause verifies does not touch this table | [`ARCHIVAL_SETTLEMENT_WRITER.md`](ARCHIVAL_SETTLEMENT_WRITER.md) | Registered 2026-08-23 at birth (rule 94 §1); prefix `SO-` checked unique against CB/CR/CT/CW/DS/GF/LV/MR/MS/MSW/OA/PF/PR/RF/RP/SA/SH/SP/TJ/VG/WI/WP/WS — **`SO` rather than `SW`**, since `SW`/`WS` are transpositions and `WS-1` is a live constraint id | **Round OPEN (2026-08-23, grounded `dev@cbba3e261`).** Unblocked by four closed upstreams: fork §7.1 **RULED 2026-08-10** (exact-min urn), the absolute-2 threshold **RATIFIED 2026-08-11**, the pass-record carrier **RULED 2026-08-18**, and the response format **LANDED 2026-08-21 (PR #522)**. **`SO-D1`…`SO-D5` and `SO-D7` CORRECTED 2026-08-24 (it re-derived a constraint the tree already enforced — the `h_close + W₂` timing is SUPERSEDED, the writer runs in the slash scheduler's per-epoch pass); `SO-D6` (reorg) **CLOSED 2026-08-24** by grounding: recompute, no alt twin, the mechanism named rather than leaned toward. `SO-D8` OPENED and assigned out of this round — the production wiring is deferred to it, so the writer has no production caller yet.** **The budget re-derivation is the round's load-bearing finding: `RF-D10` moved the binding axis rather than removing it.** Permanent chain storage per pass record falls 3,430 B → **99 B kept** (34.6×, so the ceiling that produced `k_cap = 30` now admits `k ≈ 1,040` and `λ_eff = 3` holds at `D ≈ 324 k` with 10.7× headroom), while bytes relayed rise 3,430 → **5,204 B** (`RF-D8` ruling (i) kept the ~1,920 B opening additively) and block occupancy at `k = 30` goes 103 KB → 156 KB. Block space binds first now, and it is a **penalty threshold, not a hard cap** (`CRYPTONOTE_BLOCK_GRANTED_FULL_REWARD_ZONE_V5 = 300000`): exact coverage costs ~10 KB/block at genesis `D ≈ 4,096` (free), 156 KB at `D ≈ 100 k` (free), ~506 KB at `D ≈ 324 k` (**1.7× the free zone — an economic cost, not the 'does not scale past 10⁵ pairs' wall the pre-`RF-D10` figures implied**). **Consequence that decides the schema: exact coverage is free at genesis and priced at maturity, degrading continuously with no discrete crossing event — so it is not an invariant storage may assume**, the same class as `RF-D7`'s bound-held-by-circumstance. **`SO-D1`:** the writer cannot be record-driven — the forcing case is a drawable pair whose three challenges all expire (expiry⇒miss, fork §7.3 closed 2026-08-08), which produces **zero on-chain artifacts**, so a record-driven writer leaves the row absent and the total failure settles as the most forgiving outcome; §4.2's enumerate-and-write branch is therefore adopted **unconditionally** rather than as a §7.1 fallback, making *absent ⇒ never issued ⇒ not drawable ⇒ non-observation* a theorem about the writer instead of an inference about a regime. Keyed on **issued, not drawable** (§4.4); `issued = 1` settles NonObservation and **gets a row** — auditability beats absence-consistency, because the capped regime is exactly where the teeth degrade (72 % unobservable at `k_cap = 30`) and silent degradation is unmeasured degradation. No urn state persists (§7.1 derive-don't-store, `ArchivalSealHashCache` precedent). **`SO-D2`:** key `P_id[32]‖BE(shard)[8]‖BE(E)[8]` — byte-identical in shape to `m_archival_serve_credit` so one key probes both tables, and big-endian is load-bearing (it is what makes the outer-window range scan return epochs in order); value `outcome‖passes‖issued` (3 B), no `0x00` outcome since a zero first byte is what a zero-filled cell looks like in a table whose job is making absence mean something. **Storing `issued` is the only `(m, n)`-serving byte landable today** — §7.1's wave-tail analysis makes the issued-count histogram an `(m, n)` derivation input under absolute-2, and the re-pin's other input (stressnet outage CDF) is not desk-derivable. Deriving `outcome` at read time rejected: re-deriving a consensus verdict per read is how two readers disagree. The duplicate (`outcome` is derivable from `passes`/`issued`) is deliberate and therefore gets an **uncrossable boundary**: the writer asserts `outcome == settle_epoch(passes, issued)` on the write path, alongside the `issued > 255` assert — one enforcing site, which is the only site that can produce a row. **`SO-D3`:** one derivation per settled epoch — in the slash scheduler's per-epoch pass (`SO-D7` as corrected), NOT at a separate epoch-close event — rather than incremental accumulation (which needs the close-time enumeration anyway and adds a second write path that can disagree); the ~972 k-assignment close-block spike at maturity is **priced on the record**, acceptable because it is a pure function of final chain state and off the tx-admission path, with a rule-21 reopening criterion (amortise across the final `W₂` blocks — never persist urn state). **`SO-D4`: the table is the 49th and `maxdbs` is 48** — `mdb_env_set_maxdbs(m_env, 48)` (`db_lmdb.cpp:1599`) against exactly 48 unconditional `lmdb_db_open` calls, verified by count not by reading the comment, and **the overflow fails at runtime only** (it compiles, links, and passes every test that does not open a fresh env). Ruled: `maxdbs` becomes **derived from the table list**, not bumped to 49 — hardcoding re-arms the trap for the 50th table. **Not sequenced behind the credit-wire deletion** even though that deletion frees exactly the two slots needed (`archival_attestation_witness` + its alt twin): the ruled order is writer-live-then-delete, and `maxdbs` is a ceiling that need not shrink back. **`SO-D5`:** the `failure_window.rs:93–104,181` prune const-assert **survives with its failure direction inverted** — for `m_archival_serve_credit` a pruned bit reads as a MISS and slashes honest archivers; for this table a pruned row reads as *absent* ⇒ non-observation, silently shrinking the window's own denominator. Both are wrong and one assert catches both, but the rationale string must be rewritten or a maintainer debugging a *missed* slash concludes the assert is unrelated and relaxes it. **`SO-D7`: pass evidence is not final at `h_close`.** `CHALLENGE_RESPONSE_BLOCKS = SEB/20 = 500` (`constants.rs:147`) and `assign_epoch` issues on **every block of the epoch** (`prev_hashes.len()` *is* the epoch length, `challenge_assignment.rs:262–279`, no end-of-epoch cutoff), so a challenge drawn at epoch-relative block 9,999 resolves 500 blocks into `E+1`. A writer running at `h_close` counts every in-flight challenge as zero passes — **up to `W₂/SEB` = 5 % of issuance would settle missed purely from running early**, the same slash-bias class as the §4.1 finding the drawability relocation already fixed once. Stopping issuance at `SEB − W₂` was rejected: it truncates the urn's draw window by 5 % and perturbs the redraw floor and wave-tail exposure, both **ruled** on the full-epoch assumption — re-opening a closed parameter to simplify a writer is backwards. Ruled instead: **run once the epoch's evidence is final** — **CORRECTED 2026-08-24**: the opening draft ruled a dedicated `h_close + W₂` event, and grounding showed the tree already enforces that finality, so the writer runs inside the **slash scheduler's per-epoch pass** instead. A separate scheduled event would have added a second height→epoch log and a second revert path to buy a margin already guaranteed. The lag argument below is what survives the correction; the `h_close + W₂` timing does not. **The lag is not free and `SO-D1`'s theorem gains a precondition**: between `h_close(E)` and `h_close(E) + W₂` epoch `E`'s rows are absent because unwritten, so *absent ⇒ non-observation* holds only at `h ≥ h_close(E) + W₂` and a window walk must **exclude** an unsettled epoch rather than count it — stated as a constraint on the reader, not as "the writer usually runs first" (the `RF-D7` lesson, third application). Verified rather than assumed: the **emission gather does not read this table** (pure presence cursor-walk over `m_archival_serve_credit`, `db_lmdb.cpp:7801,:7804`, written at admission), so emission timing is untouched by the lag; the slash-window walk is the consumer that must respect the precondition. **`SO-D6` CLOSED (recompute, no alt twin — the revert DELETES the rows and lets the reconnect recompute; wired in `revert_archival_slashes_at_height` across the whole span the height folded)** — settlement rows are a memoised derivation over final chain state, unlike the witness table's *received* evidence which is unreproducible on a losing branch; held open because the argument assumes a reorg crossing a close boundary always re-runs the writer before any reader sees stale rows, an ordering claim about `pop_block`/emission-gather that is **named as unread rather than asserted**. **Evidence discipline: the shadow cutover is unavailable** — the Phase-4 equivalence KAT is superseded because 2-of-3 over derived challenges is *supposed* to disagree with the self-served vin, so agreement would be evidence of a bug; the writer's proof is the forcing case as a red test (the edit that makes it red is deleting the enumeration, per rule 50), the absent-row theorem as a test, a pinned issued-count histogram doubling as the `(m, n)` sweep input, and the re-argued prune assert. **Five stale sites found while grounding, all FIXED IN THIS PR in their own commits** (§11), three pointing at landed work: the RF status header (plus two `(OPEN)` disposition headings), this index's own RF row head, `FOLLOWUPS`' coinbase-prunable text (`RF-D10` landed serve-credit-tx-prunable), a **dangling `§5.6` anchor** cited by two sites for a section that exists nowhere in the tree — invisible until the correction *quoted* it, which is the argument for restating a cited claim rather than passing the citation along — and, the dangerous one, `ARCHIVAL_CHALLENGE_MECHANISM.md` §9.6 item 2 plus `challenge.rs:9–19`, which call `verify_segment_path`/`challenge_leaf_index` fossil and name a deletion trigger that has now fired, when `RF-D8` ruling (i) made both **permanent consensus admission code** (`blockchain.cpp:5412` → `serve_credit.rs:201`) |
| **CR-D1…CR-Dn** | Pass-record **carrier** design-round dispositions: where the record's kept and pruned parts live in the tx wire | [`ARCHIVAL_PASS_RECORD_CARRIER.md`](ARCHIVAL_PASS_RECORD_CARRIER.md) | Registered 2026-08-17 at birth (rule 94 §1); prefix `CR-` passes the uniqueness check against Phase/Stage/M/Bond-PR/SP/SP-T/PR-A/PR-B/PR-E/CT/GF/S/R/F/DQ/SCE/WI/M1/CB | **Round RULED 2026-08-18** (opened 2026-08-17, grounded `dev@c92f10ed4`). Three findings moved the framing: **CR-F1** the pass record is *already a live vin* (`txin_archival_serve_credit_response`, tag `0x02`, read by `blockchain_db.cpp:265`/`:816`), so the question is migration not placement; **CR-F2** both signature legs are *already colocated* in one 3,385 B container inside the vin ⇒ inside the prefix ⇒ covered by `prefix_hash` ⇒ unprunable (`3385 = 12 + 64 Ed25519 + 3309 ML-DSA-65`), so the round is a split-and-migrate that changes the tx id, not an addition to an empty region; **CR-F3** the `pqc_auths` slot is *forbidden*, not merely occupied. **CR-D1 RESOLVED 2026-08-18 by reading — there was no tension:** `blockchain.cpp:3726` skips the count check for serve-credit txs because `:3746-3754` imposes a **stricter** rule twenty lines later (`pqc_auths` **must be empty**, error string *"signature is on the vin"*), and `:3630` skips the whole spend arm as non-spending. Serialization at `cryptonote_basic.h:620-621` is satisfied trivially via the `:605` gate. The appearance of tension came from reading `:3726` alone. Consequence: the kept Ed25519 leg **stays on the vin** — `pqc_auths` is per-input *spend* authorization and a countersignature attests to a *read*, so the slot is a category error even where permitted — and **CR-F3's blast radius dissolves** (no exemption to remove, `:3630`/`:3726`/`:3746` all unchanged). **Settled:** something must be prunable — ~88 GB/yr of mandatory baseline at `D≈324k`, `λ=3` is disqualifying. **CR-D2 RESOLVED 2026-08-18 — the round is a record PARTITION, not a signature split.** The segment layout fixes the size by arithmetic: `SEGMENT_LAYER_J = 2`, Selene/Helios widths 38/18 ⇒ `38·18·38 = 25,992` leaves; the assembler's `for layer in 1..depth` excludes the leaf layer, so depth 3 yields **two** branch layers `(18+38)·32 = 1,792 B`; and the **leaf chunk** `4·38·32 = 4,864 B` is on-wire cost despite no struct naming it — `verify_segment_path` takes `leaf_layer_scalars` as a separate parameter and `leaf_bytes_in_layer` requires the whole chunk, so a verifier can neither run without it nor derive it. Whole record ≈ **10,243 B ≈ 262 GB/yr**. **CR-O1 REJECTED** (splitting the signature legs keeps ~6,934 B ≈ 177 GB/yr — `path`, not the signature, is the dominant term); the verdict is **overdetermined**, failing at every reading of the leaf-chunk question including the smallest. **CR-O1′ survives:** leaf chunk + branch layers + ML-DSA leg pruned inside `serialize_ctsig_prunable`, keeping header + `leaf_bytes` + Ed25519 ≈ **278 B ≈ 7.1 GB/yr** (**refined to ~230 B ≈ 5.9 GB/yr by RF-D6**, which removes `segment_subroot_rk` and `leaf_index_in_segment` from the record — the partition is unchanged, the record's contents are) — enough to identify the record and check the classical signature, **not** enough to re-verify the opening, which is a design property rather than an oversight. The leg split survives as a sub-decision, and `CR-D1` supplied its placement. **Structural consequence:** a vin cannot straddle `unprunable_size` (`tx.vin` serializes wholly inside the prefix), so the pruned parts are a **parallel structure keyed to the vin**, living *inside* `serialize_ctsig_prunable` — never appended after `ctsig_prunable`, per the tripwire. `CR-F2`'s `prefix_hash`/tx-id change is priced and is the format round's to land. **Corrected in the same PR (same validation surface — both are "did we count the opening correctly?"):** `shekyl-economics-sim`'s `RESPONSE_BYTES` understated by **2.16×**; W10 still fails at bulk transit (`q* ≈ 0.274`) but the retail-egress end no longer free-rides (`margin > 0` at `q ≈ 0`). The two proxy tests are restated to assert both ends separately; whether **A5/W10** survives overall is `ARCHIVAL_WORK_PRECISION_AND_ESCALATION.md`'s to re-run. **Dissolved:** the coinbase `txin_gen` guard — under derived assignment nothing is recorded in the coinbase, so the record rides an ordinary tx. **Landed with the round regardless of outcome:** the two-path tripwire (`tests/unit_tests/tx_prunable_region_sole_occupant.cpp`) — the blob and re-serialize paths of `calculate_transaction_prunable_hash` agree only because `ctsig_prunable` is written last, a *positional* invariant that had no name and no test; an append after it diverges the two paths and throws `"tx hash cash integrity failure"` on blob-holding nodes only. **Unblocks the response-format round**, which is next and inherits one written constraint: *the leaf chunk is pruned-side by construction; a format that groups it beside `leaf_bytes` for tidiness is not disagreeing with CR-D2, it is not noticing it.* Separate PR per rule 19 (hash-equivalence and domain-separation are independent validation surfaces) |

PR-4a lands: the CB-4 blob-boundary arm (**Q11-KAT FOLLOWUPS item CLOSED** — fixture made valid under full `ver_non_input_consensus` dispatch, verdict-invariance arm under `canonical_bytes` variation, oversized-`pseudoOuts` control), the FAKECHAIN-only settlement-epoch-blocks env lever + serve-credit-bit injection RPC (e2e enablers, both fail-closed off FAKECHAIN), `LocalNodeRpc` (local-posture `PersonaIsolatedTransport`, DQ-T2.3 choice site), the `SweptFeeInputs`/`ClaimOperands` designation-event witness (PR-3's named forward item, landed with the seam as scoped — both step-2 runtime refusal arms deleted) + the CB-3 dispatch seam (`engine/claim_dispatch.rs`: read-only identity projection → orchestrate → persist-before-dispatch seal → posture→submit choke point; the last claim-surface staging allows deleted), the staker regtest harness up to the daemon submit gap (production transfer → P-scan discovery → bond assembly → dispatch, with the `Malformed` tripwire pinning the gap), and two harness-surfaced production bug fixes with unit KATs (payment path encapsulated to `montgomery(view_pk)` — cross-wallet payments were unrecoverable; P-scan exhaustiveness fetches full tx bodies — pruned FCMP++ spends can never re-hash to their committed hashes). **PR-4a review round (2026-07-12, high-effort workflow review — 29 verified findings, all addressed in one round; record in `EMISSION_CLAIM_BUILDER.md` §8)**: the SEB override converted to explicit **arming** (unarmed processes — every wallet, every public-net daemon — run the genesis schedule and warn once on an ignored lever; the daemon arms only on FAKECHAIN, refuses on the lever's presence off it, refuses invalid values loudly instead of clamping, and pins the effective schedule into the fakechain datadir — reopen-under-a-different-schedule is a loud refusal); the CB-3 seam gained **persist-before-dispatch** (`PendingEmissionClaim`, pending-post block v4: bytes + fee-gindex reservation into the shared `reserved_gindexes` union + claimed epochs; one live claim per persona = in-flight dedup, `ClaimPending` refusal; retirement/resubmit = the named claim-driver slice) and lost its rotation side effect (claimant id via the read-only `PersonaIdentityOf` projection — activation would invalidate in-flight handles and wipe a retired ephemeral persona); typed taxonomies: `NoSpendableFunding` (empty eligible set ≠ zero-value shortfall; the `ClaimFeeInputsRequired` mapping keys on the typed arm, not `available == 0`) and `OutputNotYetDrained{gindex}` (the harness's substring match on a rendered error deleted); the P-scan full-body fetch names the storage-pruned-daemon cause + remedy; credential grammar authority-scoped at the source (`SimpleRequestRpc`) with `LocalNodeRpc`'s pin moved to `IpAddr::is_loopback`; consolidations: single `DaemonEngine::fetch_scannable_block_in_form` override point, `BroadcastSubmitter::local` (three copies deleted), `EpochCloseInputs::close_view` (FFI lockstep retired), `EmissionClaimReceipt` embeds the reply, shared e2e `create_wallet`, mtime-gated `pscan_until`, `tokio::join!`ed seam reads; | **TJ-A…TJ-G** | Test≡job + pruning-is-upstream sequencing round (two maintainer rulings 2026-07-29): **R1** the retention challenge must demand what a client demands — the SHARD, never a ~3 KB opening (the payload asymmetry is what manufactured A5's free-rider margin); **R2** the market's product is set-B scarcity, which exists only when pruned-daemon mode ships — today every daemon retains every leaf forever (segment-freeze fact §2.5, and that retention is *consensus-required by the current verify path*, which reads arbitrary local leaf scalars — the coupling runs both ways). Two-legged necessity: pruning without a real test pays proxies re-fetching from the foundation; a real test without pruning pays everyone for bytes they already hold. Sequencing resolution: challenge-VERIFY semantics are consensus (genesis-frozen; must verify responder-supplied leaf+path against `R_k`, which set A gives every node) — the pruning MODE is node-local and ships post-genesis without coordination (rule 75). Re-classifies W10/A5 as a pre-pruning artifact (machinery retained, re-runs at shard payload); dissolves reopen (d) as premature; pauses the PD probe | [`ARCHIVAL_TEST_EQUALS_JOB_SEQUENCING.md`](ARCHIVAL_TEST_EQUALS_JOB_SEQUENCING.md) | Registered 2026-07-29 at birth (rule 94 §1); prefix `TJ-` passes the uniqueness check (no family mints `TJ<letter/digit>` tokens) | Round 0 CLOSED; **Round 1 OPEN on the witness problem** (§8, 2026-07-29). Ledger's three legs now LOCATED, not pooled: leg 1 price-contingent (within-class per F-1 — decisive ~11× metered-retail, the cheap-transit watch-cell the §5.3 reopen aims at was MARGINAL ~1.1× at one challenge per epoch and is **DECISIVE ~3.3× since the 2-of-3 ruling set λ = 3** (TJ-A2d's lever, measured 2026-08-11), inverted flat-rate); leg 2 `T_risk` structural and **MEASURED** (`q_risk* ≈ 0.1011` +reward / 0.2792 bond-only, break-even fetch $0.0004/GB, PR #379); leg 3 **build cost SPLIT into two incompatible quantities** (§5.7, an equivocation cleared): **3a acquisition BYTES** — the free-rider pays perpetually what the honest archiver sank once (`honest = B + N·S` vs `free-rider = N·B`), a horizon correction that converts the cheap-transit coin-flip into a standing asymmetry, but priced in transferred bytes so it goes QUIET at flat-rate; **3b engineering/operational LABOR** — the honest path's marginal engineering is zero while the free-rider builds a subsystem that does not exist (retention suppression, challenge interception, live source map over a churning bonded set, deadline-bound fetch with failover), engineer-months against single-digit dollars per bond-year, denominated in labor so flat-rate does NOT zero it — but explicitly payoff-PROPORTIONAL, commoditizable by one published implementation, and no defense against a determined builder, so it raises the threshold at which tooling gets written and must NOT be leaned on. Flat-rate cell therefore rests on `T_risk` + TJ-C's deadline + a labor threshold nobody should lean on ⇒ §8.3's gate is LOAD-BEARING. Gates landed (PR #378): `tj_g_red_challenge_names_every_segment_leaf` red on challenge scope (rejects every `k` < 25,992), `tj_f_forged_material_does_not_verify` green on soundness, `work_sums_over_exactly_the_answered_pairs` welding the ledger's per-`(P,s,E)` granularity; TJ-F's liveness face carried as a **type-level obligation** on TJ-A's output (entry point takes response + `R_k`, no store handle), not a test. Round 1 inherits the branch asymmetry as **CLOSED** — sampling fails three independent ways (cost ledger §5.6 at ~1:260, 8C §4's Θ(m) zero-storage+reacquire text, and CI) — and puts its design questions at **CHALLENGER INTEGRITY**: **WITNESS POPULATION RULED 2026-07-29: MINERS, not the bonded set** — the bonded-set draw and its two rounds of mitigation (co-holder exclusion, denial-tolerant quorum, `1/r` denial-to-monopolize) are RETRACTED as design and RETAINED as diagnosis: they engineered around a threat the population choice manufactured (the `reground-the-whether` failure — two review rounds verified the choice's execution, never the choice). Miners carry both required properties ALREADY PROVEN: **indifference** by §12.11.1 Leg 1 (`miner_fee_income = total_fees − burned_amount` is invariant to the staker share across the whole `n` domain, pinned by `escalating_the_share_never_touches_miner_income`; serve credit only redistributes a budget it cannot resize) and **liveness** by PoW (selection IS proof of being online — the only population with the property; an offline witness's stall is indistinguishable from a denial, which was the prior scheme's failure mode). **TJ-A3 COLLAPSES**: verification is deterministic (recompute sub-root, compare `R_k`), honest witnesses cannot disagree and `P` cannot serve two valid responses, so disagreement is EVIDENCE (witness lied, or `P` served selectively — newly named and detectable), majority resolves, outlier is a signal. Remaining: TJ-A1 miner selection window (set now enumerable from block headers), TJ-A2 miner/bonder overlap (NOT excludable — G-1 forbids linking miner identity to persona; quorum now defends only the overlap FRACTION, a far weaker job), TJ-A2b witness obligation (paid = compatible; **block-validity = RULED OUT, it puts a 3.33 MB Tor transfer on the mining critical path — the invalid-blocks class**), TJ-A2c quorum size where the trade **INVERTS** (raising `n` DILUTES the deterrent: honest pays `n` egress, free-rider `n` egress + ONE ingress, so the differential is constant while shared cost grows — three-way tension, two of three push `n` small), TJ-A2d the escape to evaluate FIRST: `k`-of-`n` over SEQUENTIAL challenges (one witness each) preserves the ratio, spreads miner bandwidth, and **multiplies the deterrent** since `CHALLENGES_PER_EPOCH = 1` today (`constants.rs:11`) — a measurable claim re-runnable through `tj_shard_payload_report` by changing one operand, and the lever that could lift §5.7c's marginal cheap-transit cell. **MECHANISM RULED (§9, 2026-07-29): THE TEST IS A READ** — miners use the same mechanism any wallet uses, `P` cannot distinguish test from read, so passing requires *actually being able to serve* (a distinguishable challenge would let `P` fast-path tests while real requests rot — teaching-to-the-test, the anti-shim principle at the protocol layer). ARCHAEOLOGY: the ~3 KB opening was **forced, not chosen** — `P` self-attested in its own tx, and an artifact that fits in a tx can only prove CAPABILITY, never SERVICE; service needs a counterparty, so the rulings are FORCED IN SEQUENCE (test≡job ⇒ off-chain serve ⇒ third-party attester ⇒ indifferent+live = miners ⇒ indistinguishable request). L14's read-credit **SUBSUMED** (it bridged two events test≡job collapses into one; its 65% saving was on artificial tests and there are none left) ⇒ cadence **UNIFORM by construction**, which is correct not fallback (paid to be ABLE to serve; popularity-weighted testing would under-check exactly the unchecked holdings). Remaining = **TWO items**: (i) the client-facing read/serve protocol is TJ-B's real surface — **unblocks SP-T3** (its deferral was 'consumer-less infrastructure, no byte-defined payload'; this supplies payload=shard + consumer=drawn miner) and promotes response semantics to genesis-frozen; SP-T4b's absent producer changes hands from `P`-assembles-opening to witness-submits-receipt; (ii) **TJ-A5 DISSOLVED (2026-07-29)**, three findings: the evidence had NO CONSUMER (no tribunal; the m-of-n window over independent draws IS the adjudication — statistical over repetition, not forensic over one event; ephemeral-key candidate dropped, the read stays plain); the timely-commitment cost-binding is **RETRACTED** (the response is deterministic so ANY co-holder computes the commitment — ~100 bytes across the free-rider's link, and honest/free-riding commitments are byte-identical ⇒ zero adjudication information; the keyed HMAC variant fails on HORIZON — one key-share unlocks unlimited generation, §5.7a inverted — and its second-order effect is OUTSOURCING, since sharing `k_P` = handing over rewards/unbond = the helper IS `P`'s storage); what binds is **TOPOLOGY** — the witness reaches `P` only via `P`'s onion rendezvous, so the bytes traverse `P`'s link even as a dumb pipe (`n·I − S` enforced by path position, no artifact from `P`; the 'inseparable signature' question is answered no-and-unneeded — bytes are self-authenticating against `R_k`, relaying at full freight IS priced service). **WINDOW ASYMMETRY INVERTED (§9.6)**: denying honest `P` needs ~11/13 hostile (`f ≈ 0.85`), sustaining zero-service `P` needs ~3/13 friendly (`f ≈ 0.23`/shard) — outage-absorption ≡ attestation-forgiveness (`n − m + 1`), so the `(m,n)` re-pin gains objective 3: **ATTESTATION-RESISTANCE**, bribe priced against the zero-service reward take, portfolio (4,096 independent windows) swept via PD-D's predicate. SIZING (§9.5): honest egress CANCELS (validating deliverable 2's model), reward cancels and dwarfs costs 389–2598× so there is **no viability ceiling** (headroom to `n ≈ 747`), `n ≈ 10` buys a 10× deterrent margin, and miner bandwidth is an aggregate ÷ population (~9 KB/s per miner across 1,000) whose real constraint is miner-set concentration. **ONE OPEN GATE the round does not own:** is `q ≥ 0.10` forceable on a 3.33 MB Tor-borne fetch inside `CHALLENGE_RESOLUTION_BLOCKS` — PD-F-2's dispersion measurement; forceable ⇒ the fork has two live options, not ⇒ receipt is the only branch and Round 1 is a design of the attestation (§8.3) |

REVIEW ROUND 2026-08-08 (same branch): moving the lock from the row to a parameter dropped two implicit properties, both restored in-PR rather than deferred — (a) `reconcile_send_journal` gained **step 3, evidence-bound release** (`mark_presumed_dead` for a row the ledger refutes: a carried input spent by a foreign txid, or every carried input out of view past `ledger.height() >= dispatched_at_height`), restoring the ledger-evidence bound `held_submits` lost when it became journal-only — without it the only other exit is the confirm edge, so such a row alarmed and pinned its tx bytes forever; (b) the lock set became the newtype `InFlightSpendLocks` — renamed off the opaque spec tag `F14`, which said nothing about what is locked — with **no `Default`** and `SendJournalBlock::spend_locks` as its only constructor, so a lock-blind `&Default::default()` call no longer compiles, and the bare `LedgerBlockExt::balance` that invited one was **deleted** (`WalletLedgerExt::balance` derives the locks itself and is now the only balance API). Also: the bundle `format_version` gate moved ahead of the postcard body decode, so this PR's own version bump can actually fire on the stale files it exists for (`UnsupportedFormatVersion`, not an opaque framing error). Still open from the round: SJ-DQ-5 two-tier deletion, SJ-DQ-7 annotation half (verified: `cargo test` on the four wallet crates + `cargo bench -p shekyl-engine-state --bench balance_iai`). |

The method defect behind it — conflating *read* with *consumed*, when the rule-18 resume seam round-trips whole records — was applied to the rest of the table: every kept row re-verified against the consumer definition, docstrings re-worded to "no live consumer" naming the round-trip. REVIEW ROUND 2026-08-12: the adopt half is the correction — raising alone burned the cursor past a live on-chain bond while `bonded_slots` is the ONLY input that holds a persona in `derive_forward_slots`, so under Model D (seed gone after `assemble`) the bond became un-unbondable for the wallet's life, with the proving `bond_post_matches` row still sitting durably in the same seal the raise had just read. A durably-RETIRED slot is the one `Present` arm #4 refuses to adopt (re-arming it is the forever-derive problem arm #2 kills) — it still burns the cursor, which is why the explicit `monotone_current_slot(Some(_))` call survives adoption subsuming it. Same round: **arm #2 now burns before it drops** — `retain` is destructive, so a cursor rolled back below a retired slot outside the lookahead was free to re-offer it, an SA-R-6 hole arm #4 cannot reach at that distance. Density pinned as an invariant (`monotone_selection_is_dense_no_gap_reachable`) so a future edit introducing sparsity fails a test rather than silently breaking the restore reconstruction — restated in the review round to pin the **allocation sequence** (what the from-seed probe's "terminate at the first empty slot" rule actually rests on, since it reads on-chain history) rather than the live `bonded_slots` hint, which arms #2/#3 deliberately GC into a non-contiguous view; the added second leg shows the sequence stays dense *despite* that GC because `p_slot` carries the high-water mark. **Separate slice (reopen):** a cursor rolled back *beyond* the lookahead — a fresh restore-from-seed opens as a non-staker (`staking_enabled=false`) and derives/scans nothing — needs a widening forward probe under Model D (`ARCHIVAL_PERSONA_LOOKAHEAD = 2`). **Reachability corrected in the review round:** the pre-review text claimed bond posting was inert in production ("`persist_bond_record` is `test`/`test-helpers`-only, no RPC staking entry") and rested the deferral on that. It is FALSE — `handlers.rs` routes `"stake"` → `lifecycle::stake` (no `cfg`, gated only on `Capability::Full`) → `Engine::first_stake` (`pub async fn`, no `cfg`) → `persist_bond_record` at `bond_orchestrator.rs:667` → assemble + pending seal. Bond posting is production-reachable TODAY, so every SA-R-6 hole is live, not future — which is why the adopt/burn corrections above landed in-PR instead of deferring. The from-seed probe remains deferred on its own merits (a widening derive-and-scan under Model D is a distinct validation surface, rule 19), not on an inertness that does not hold. Recorded as "none found, per-surface evidence" with no rule-21 reopen owed on that axis; the §4 FCMP++/BP+ ZK-soundness risk is the different, already-recorded axis). **UPDATE 2026-08-13 — bond-watch follow-on (`feat/rescan-slot-reconstruction`, post-#458): the from-seed reconstruction BUILT** — the deferred widening probe landed as the principal scan's bond watch, per the user's ruling that any full rescan should always set the pointer. `StakingBlock` v2 (`STAKING_BLOCK_VERSION` 2, `WALLET_LEDGER_FORMAT_VERSION` 17) adds the **probe-id cache** (`persona_id_cache`: public persona canonical ids for `{bonded} ∪ {cursor..=cursor+W}`, `ARCHIVAL_PERSONA_PROBE_WINDOW = 32`, derived once at open while the seed is transiently in scope — ids are pure functions of the seed, so every later open and the credential-less `rescan_blockchain` load the map at zero keygen cost, honoring the rule-76 floor while staying UNCONDITIONAL for every wallet) and the **sighting rows** (`bond_sightings`: slot → first-sighted height, redacted-Debug persona-history discipline). The refresh/rescan producer matches `Input::BondPost` observations (single-owner lift factored to `engine::bond_watch`, shared with the P-scan extractor) against the watch and emits slot-resolved sightings on `ScanResult`; the merge adopts each sighted slot into `bonded_slots` (sorted insert), records the first-sighting height, re-arms `staking_enabled`, and raises the monotone cursor — positive evidence only, sound on any coverage, no exhaustiveness token. Two soundness bridges: arm #3 evaluates sighted slots with the height-gated `reconcile(id, sighting_height)` (a pscan seal whose coverage predates the sighting reads `OutsideCovered` — a probe-adopted real bond cannot be GC'd as phantom; coverage past it with no match = reorged-out, dropped correctly with its row; `Present` supersedes and prunes the row), and the first-stake W2 resume guard refuses `AlreadyStaked` on a persisted sighting (a lagging P-scan otherwise read the probe-adopted slot as the W2 phantom and minted a duplicate JoinMarket post). Depth > W converges across open+rescan cycles (`ceil(depth/W)`); the watch sees only what the principal scan covers (birthday semantics). **UPDATE 2026-08-13 — review round on the bond-watch build: the absence evidence is now per-persona.** The height-gated bridge alone was not airtight — the pscan seal's match set is complete only over personas watched while coverage advanced, so a probe-adopted bond below an already-advanced frontier read `AbsentWithinCovered` and was permanently GC'd (stuck funds; two routes: restore-then-rescan with an old seal, and mid-session adoption the spawn-time actor never watches). Fixed structurally: per-persona **watch floors** persisted in the seal (`PScanState::watch_floors`, `PSCAN_STATE_VERSION` 8→9, `ScanStepResult::watched_personas` feeds them at ingest) and `PReconcileSet::reconcile` refuses absence below a persona's floor / for an unfloored persona (`Present` never gated). Companion hardening: merge-side reorg hygiene drops sighting rows at/above the fork (`discard_sightings_at_or_above`; re-mined posts re-sight at canonical height — also heals the first-stake `AlreadyStaked` wedge in-session), committed sighting rows are immutable (batch-min in `adopt_bond_sightings`; no cross-batch lowering), the refresh producer aborts `ReorgStorm` on a divergence past the rewind budget instead of scanning blind, `sightings_in` adopts JoinMarket posts only (accrual-filter parity), membership-not-order insert on `bonded_slots`, and mid-session recovery is a surfaced state (`recovery_pending_reopen` on `staking_info`/`get_wallet_info`; `-29504 STAKE_RECOVERED_PENDING_REOPEN` from `first_stake`/`start_pscan` — Model D cannot spawn the actor mid-session). Efficiency: open-time id derivation is cache-first + identity-only (`derive_archival_p_identity_pk`, byte-identical pin). Residual (funds-safe, FOLLOWUPS reopen): a below-floor sighting is retained-not-corroborated until a pscan floor-lowering backfill mode exists (distinct validation surface, rule 19). **Bond watch MERGED #463 (2026-08-14; second review round: unreadable-seal watch gating — an `Err` from the pscan seal disables the watch for the session since the retired refusal set is unknown, `Ok(None)` fresh-restore stays armed; the rescan orphaned-sighting mechanism declined with the residual's second entry path documented; seven decomposition-ratchet failures resolved structurally by extracting eight inline test suites to `#[path]` EXCLUDE'd siblings).** **SA-6 IN FLIGHT** (`feat/sa6-cbom-close`): CBOM §1 (primitives+RNG) / §2 (six surfaces) transcribed with at-source re-verification, §4 (curve-based ZK risk register) written, §6 closed — audit-status column, infra PQ posture, and the release-artifact signing gap filed as a rule-21 reopen with a `RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md` enforcement row; four stale GATE6 C-1 sites corrected to DISCHARGED (#277).
