Prefix `Q12-` passes the uniqueness check; the round's first draft used bare `D-N`/`DQ-N`/`U-N`, **all three of which collide** — `D-1…D-7` are this arc's own design decisions and `DQ-1…DQ-8` belong to the principal-stake round — so every identifier is `Q12-`-prefixed. Scoped per [`26-sub-pr-design-discipline`](../../.cursor/rules/26-sub-pr-design-discipline.mdc) (multi-round, FFI boundary, persisted layout), cited explicitly as that rule requires. | **LANDED (as of 2026-08-13).** UPDATE 2026-08-13: Q12-U3 landed (`feat/q12-u3-once-at-origin`): once-at-origin roll at `daemon_submit::relay_tx` (not `source.is_nil()` — that would re-roll pool local re-relays), `MIXED_ELIGIBILITY_PCT_HUNDREDTHS = 5000`, `/get_stem_tallies` rows carry `zone`. `origin_zone` still has no routing consumer (as of that date, HF re-validation preservation was the only production read; a second, TIMING read landed 2026-08-25 — see below). Arrival-leg `t_core` witness remains FOLLOWUPS. UPDATE 2026-08-13 (review): `send_txs` requires a `zone_route` token only `once_at_origin_route` can construct. `origin_zone` fourth consumer named: Q12-D6a isolation arm; reopen for rule-15 if that run ships without a reader. **That reopen is DISCHARGED 2026-08-25 and can no longer fire** — `local_relay_base` (`tx_pool.cpp`) reads the field on every relay pass to pick an origin's re-broadcast parameter class (`DAEMON_RELAY_PRIVACY.md` §92.5c item 3), which is a reader besides the preservation path and independent of that run. The row's "still has no routing consumer" is unaffected: the new read selects a wait, not a route. `on_relay_tx` / missed-nudge residual absorption recorded, not closed. **Was PARTLY LANDED (2026-08-12)** — Q12-U1 + Q12-U2 + Q12-U4 shipped in one PR; Q12-D5a RULED once-at-origin the same day; next unit was IMPLEMENT once-at-origin. **Read the D5a paragraph below as superseded on its conditionality**: it ends "Q12-D4 is therefore RULED CONDITIONALLY", and the condition is now settled — once-at-origin, so Q12-D4's cancellation holds outright. Q12-U2 turned the absorbing process ON (before it, a diverted tx returned to clearnet after one hop via `forward` -> the pool loop, so the once-at-origin/per-hop fork was UNOBSERVABLE and cost nothing either way); the ruling deletes the duplicate, since coherence already decides the zone and holds it while a per-arrival roll re-decides it, and the accumulated re-decisions ARE the absorption. **The telemetry-before-value gate is DISSOLVED, not satisfied** — the absorbed fraction is an artifact of per-arrival rolling, so under once-at-origin there is no `alpha` to measure and `p` needs no instrument first (indifference at 0.5 stands, as a deployment choice). An implementer must NOT rebuild that gate. Owed by the implementing unit: `p_own = p` rather than 1, which makes the `require_usable=false` caller conditional — §30.5 fail-closed SURVIVES (it governs an unusable CHOSEN zone, not whether the roll happens), so roll-said-anon-then-unusable (fail closed) and roll-said-clearnet (by design) must be DISTINGUISHABLE AT THE SITE; and `MIXED_ELIGIBILITY_PER_HOP_PCT_HUNDREDTHS` is renamed in the SAME change, because `PER_HOP` beside a once-at-origin roll is the name/mechanism disagreement that hides the next re-derivation. Origin-classified telemetry LANDED as collection-zone labels on `/get_stem_tallies` (not `origin_zone`); it VERIFIES coherence and is the isolation arm's instrument — not a precondition for `p`. See PR #460. The origin-zone field, the receive path, and the `relay_method::forward` deletion landed together. **`origin_zone` still has no routing consumer** — it is written at first arrival (`add_tx` does not revise it on a method upgrade) and preserved across re-validation. Q12-U3 labelled stem-tally rows by **collection** zone; that is not a reader of this field. U2 first claimed the pool re-relay loop was that consumer and rewrote it to route stems by origin; **retracted before push** — a `stem` reaches that loop only at embargo expiry, so its `zone::public_` literal is the Dandelion++ fluff exit (§59.1), not a leak, and re-stemming there would have let entries re-stem instead of diffusing. Q-12's disposition closed by the **deletion** branch Q12-D1 named — `forward` had no producers left, so `CRYPTONOTE_FORWARD_DELAY_BASE`/`_AVERAGE`, Rust `ForwardDelay`, and `shekyl_dandelionpp_forward_delay_seconds` are gone rather than derived. §89.2's per-zone `hop` premise is now shipped, which is what made Q12-U3's coherence-verification instrument answerable; D5a then dissolved the measure-`p`-first gate, and U3 shipped `p = 0.5` without measuring `α`. Still open and blocked on the `t_core` harness: the arrival-to-record end-to-end witness (FOLLOWUPS). *Design as opened, 2026-08-10:* **OPEN — design, nothing implemented.** Two decisions ruled at opening: **Q12-D1** Q-12 is a CONSEQUENCE of this round, not a half of it — the mechanism's structure changes, not only its inputs: anon-arrived traffic stays in-zone, stems and fluffs there under OutboundOnly, and the bridge MOVES to whichever peer relays that fluff publicly. `forward` is assigned at arrival purely on `zone != public_`, so if that assignment stops the class is unreachable. Rejecting the derive-now option is therefore not "wrong later" but refusing to derive a constant for a class that may not exist — §63.2 in its purest form. Q-12 closes one of three ways: by DELETION (no constant), by deriving later against a defined bridge point, or as a NEW derivation at the new site carrying the old rationale. **Q12-D1a**: the anonymity-set rationale survives the move and improves — a bridging node still leaks which anon peer fed it, but an anon fluff reaches MANY peers, so many can bridge and a delay at each randomises which goes first; better than today's single-bridger-with-a-delay, and an argument for the field beyond fixing coherence; **Q12-D2** reinstate the field WITHOUT reversing §89.2's back-out — the embargo arming site is *told* the zone and needs no field, the pool re-relay must *remember* it because it runs after the moment that knew; two consumers, two correct answers (§89.8.6). Ground findings verified at [`6bc65d2a9`](https://github.com/Shekyl-Foundation/shekyl-core/commit/6bc65d2a9091d3da5d6d8d906ed3420c250b947d): the delay draws only for `relay_method::forward` (`tx_pool.cpp:310-312`), which is exactly the class re-relayed to `zone::public_` as a literal (`cryptonote_core.cpp:1091`), so **the forward delay IS the bridge timing**; `tx_pool.cpp:312` is the ONLY `random_poisson_seconds` caller and `random_poisson_subseconds` has **none**, so deliverable 3 retires the whole `random_poisson_duration` template rather than one call site — and it is **SPLIT OUT of this round** (§5.1) because it dies in EVERY branch: if `forward` goes the call site goes with it, and if `forward` survives F-2/F-4 already settled the family must be memoryless. A deletion whose justification is already complete should not inherit an open round's latency; `FORWARD_DELAY_BASE` still has no consumer. **Q12-Q8 is the round's FIRST question and the rest are downstream of it: after anon-arrived traffic stays in-zone, where does the clearnet bridge happen and does it need a delay?** (numbering records the order raised, not answered — renumbering registered identifiers to flatter the reading order would be worse than the note). Open: Q12-Q1 field width/placement, **Q12-Q2 ANSWER EARLY** — pool-loop routing when the origin zone is unusable, flagged because the two candidate rules differ in exactly the hazardous direction (originated FAILS CLOSED, relayed FALLS THROUGH to clearnet) and inheriting the wrong one silently sends anonymity-arrived traffic public, which is the defect the field exists to fix, failing quietly with no error and only the wrong network as a symptom; Q12-Q3 whether `forward` survives as a class, Q12-Q4 `λ`/`N` grounding (inbound is not outbound), Q12-Q5 whether the inherited "2+" target is adequate at all, **Q12-Q6 NARROWED — the family half is landed** (PR #431: memoryless at the unchanged 22 s, `random_poisson_duration` deleted with its header; 2.38x phase-averaged on F-4's own instrument, 2.01x at phase 0 as the floor, and 0.9184 at the inherited draw's worst supported phase against a flat 0.1248, reproducing F-4's "up to 93 %" independently), leaving the MEAN alone — and that is downstream of Q12-Q8 rather than of measurement technique; Q12-Q7 whether `FORWARD_DELAY_BASE` dies. **Q12-Q8's answer changes the delay's OBJECTIVE**, which must be settled before anyone derives against the old one: today one bridger makes "2+ incoming connections could have sent it" plausible, but after the field an anon fluff reaches many peers and each can bridge — with no delay, first-to-clearnet is decided by network latency and leaks the fluff source's neighbourhood (a TOPOLOGY leak, a different failure than the current delay defends), while a delay at each makes it a race among independent draws. So the objective becomes **decouple the first bridger from network distance**, whose sufficient condition is a delay spread large against inter-node latency spread — plausibly HUNDREDS OF MILLISECONDS, not tens of seconds, since it need only dominate propagation jitter rather than manufacture an anonymity set alone. This disposes of Q12-Q5 favourably (the set is now the fluff reach, so "2+ = one bit" stops being the target) and sharpens Q12-D1a: the requirement WEAKENS, so a derivation against the old objective would over-provision by ~2 orders of magnitude — the concrete cost of Q12-D1's rejected option. **Q12-D3 RULED 2026-08-10 — zone is a ROUTING CHOICE WITH A COST, not a property a transaction inherits.** Both directions are the same kind of thing: a selectable path with a latency price. One rule — at entry pick a zone with some probability, coherence holds it there, the zone's `hop` prices the path. **It DELETES rather than decides**: `relay_method::forward` (it means only "arrived somewhere other than clearnet" — provenance — and under one rule provenance stops being a routing input, answering Q12-Q3 as *no*); the fall-through branch (an unusable zone is simply not selectable, like an unavailable peer — already `select_anonymity(require_usable=true)`'s semantics); and **Q12-Q2 entirely**, since no special case remains to give a rule to. **Q12-D3a, verified at source**: the exit already exists and is NOT at the stem — `still_stemming` is false for `fluff`, so a fluffed tx bypasses coherence and reaches clearnet regardless of origin, and a peer receiving an anon fluff classes it `fluff` and relays publicly next hop. Tor→clearnet bridging runs through R-1's `tx_relay` exit today. **So `p_out = 0` is a legitimate setting that still buys the whole unification** — "bridge both directions" does NOT require a nonzero second probability, and whether stem-phase divert-out is worth enabling is a separate, smaller question. **Q12-D3b**: symmetric mechanism, ASYMMETRIC parameters — `p_in` sized for dilution (clearnet volume dwarfs anon, so R-1's 2 % is a large absolute inflow into a small zone, which is what mixes it), `p_out` for whatever the round decides; they must not be set equal for symmetry's sake. **Q12-D3c**: the latency is already priced — per-zone `hop` carries 1750 ms interim against 175 ms, so the unification adds no cost axis, it makes zone selection READ the existing one. **Q12-D4 RULED 2026-08-10 — `p` CANCELS; the privacy requirement is the EQUALITY, not the value.** The parameter looked hard because it was being derived while holding the inherited rule that ALL originated traffic goes to the anonymity zone. Drop that and it falls out. With origination rate `A`, a node's stem-phase relay rate is `A/q` (each tx traverses `1/q` stem nodes). Current rule (`p_own=1, p_relay=p`): `precision_anon = q/(q+p)` = **0.909** at `q=0.2, p=0.02`, and parity needs `p=1`. Unified rule (same `p` both classes): `precision_anon = p·A/(p·A + p·A/q) = q/(1+q)` = **0.1667 for EVERY `p`** — computed, not asserted — which is exactly the floor a clearnet-only node already has (`A/(A + A/q)`). **The shipped configuration is the WORST point in the family** (`p_own=1, p_relay=0.02` is maximal sorting). So zone selection stops carrying origin information at all and `p` becomes a DEPLOYMENT parameter rather than a privacy one. **Stated precisely, because the obvious phrasing overclaims**: `q/(1+q)` IS the C1 floor — the same floor clearnet already has and §6.9 concedes as irreducible — so zone selection stops being a SORTING axis without becoming a PROTECTION; both zones now sit AT the floor instead of one sitting above it. An earlier draft said "removed rather than covered", which reads as a leak closing, and a reviewer who checks finds 0.1667 is exactly where clearnet already was. **The stronger and correct claim is about the mechanism: `p` cancels, so the parameter CANNOT BE SET WRONG.** Every other axis this arc made safe was made safe by provisioning at a quantile (hardware §78, shape §80, depth §82), each needing a value a later reviewer could re-derive against typical rather than worst case — and §81.4 names why that is dangerous (too low has a feedback channel, TOO HIGH IS THE ERROR NOTHING REPORTS). This axis has no value to get wrong, so there is nothing for a well-intentioned reviewer with a benchmark to propose changing: the first axis in the arc where §81.4's asymmetry has no purchase, structurally rather than by being guarded. It holds per node at any origination ratio: a heavy originator still has a poor floor (C1, inherent) but the zone choice no longer makes it worse. **Dissolves Q12-Q4 and Q12-Q5** — no anonymity-set size to derive `p` against, so `λ`/`N` are not needed for it and "is 2+ the right target" has no target left to grade. `p=1` (Tor-only) is the natural default for a node running an anon zone at all — the operator configured Tor to avoid IP exposure, and clearnet traffic exposes their IP at the floor; intermediate `p` buys latency against some IP exposure, which is the operator's own trade rather than a stranger's. **Q12-D4a — two checks owed before ratification**: (1) PIN which population the entry roll applies to — the `1/q` ratio assumes stem-phase, verified at source for current code (`still_stemming`, §60's "pre-fluff traffic only"); if it ever applied to all relayed traffic the cancellation still holds (ratio-preserving) but zone VOLUMES differ by ~1000×, which is §60.4's error in a new place and exactly what the surviving cancellation would hide; (2) PRICE `p=1`'s throughput cost — **the check that can still MOVE the design**: it makes the anon zone the node's only relay path, so that zone's capacity and latency become the node's throughput (1750 ms interim `hop` against clearnet's 175 ms). If the zone cannot carry the volume the natural response is an intermediate `p`, which splits the node's traffic across two zones — the cancellation SURVIVES that (each stream carries the correct mix) but the node's IP then appears on clearnet for `1−p` of its traffic, **exactly the exposure the operator ran Tor to avoid**. So `p` is privacy-neutral BETWEEN ZONES and NOT privacy-neutral FOR THE OPERATOR; calling it "a deployment parameter" invites reading it as costless, and it is not — its cost lands on the operator who sets it rather than on the strangers whose transactions they relay. **Q12-D5 RETRACTED THE DAY IT WAS WRITTEN — there is no partition.** Coherence chooses the zone once at origin and holds it, so a stem path is ZONE-UNIFORM and never alternates: nothing to bridge, no component severed. What exists is two OVERLAPPING graphs (clearnet spanning `N`, anon spanning `t·N`, every Tor-capable node in both); `p` selects which graph a tx uses, it does not connect them. So the endpoints have no structural exclusion and `p=1` is not a defection at the stem layer. What survives, restated: a Tor-stemming tx's set is `t·N` because the anon graph spans `t·N` — adoption-dependent, not `p`-dependent. **Q12-D5a — the finding the retraction uncovered, and it is UPSTREAM OF Q12-D4 ITSELF.** Per-hop rolling plus coherence is a ONE-WAY ABSORBING process: a tx at stem position `j` is still on clearnet with probability `(1−p)^j`, so clearnet traffic skews toward low `j` — toward the origin, and position 0 IS the origin. With `α` the absorbed fraction: `precision_clearnet = q/(q+1−α)` RISES above `q/(1+q)`. Computed at `q=0.2`: **0.4573 at `p=0.25`, 0.8649 at `p=0.5`, 1.0000 at `p=1`** — against the 0.909 defect this round exists to remove. **So absorption does not weaken Q12-D4's cancellation, it REPRODUCES THE ORIGINAL DEFECT ON THE OTHER ZONE**: the zone carrying only fresh originations becomes the one that identifies them, R-1's oracle mirrored. It breaks the cancellation because the classes are absorbed at different effective rates — the origin's own tx is always fresh (`α=0`) while its relayed traffic is partly absorbed — so nothing divides out. Under ONCE-AT-ORIGIN the cancellation is exact: both zones 0.1667 at every `p`, verified. The earlier concavity finding (benefit `1−(1−p)^{1/q}` saturating while cost accrues, cost-per-unit-benefit +61 % across the range) is the SAME CULPRIT seen from the other side — an artifact of absorbing semantics; under once-at-origin both terms go linear and the ratio is flat at 7.88, indifference exact. Two independent analyses, one cause. **Q12-D4 is therefore RULED CONDITIONALLY** and bannered as such *(SUPERSEDED 2026-08-12: the condition is settled — Q12-D5a ruled once-at-origin, so the cancellation holds outright)*, and **Q12-D4a check 1 is RE-FILED AT CORRECTNESS SEVERITY**: it was written as a volume detail, then read as a value justification, and is in fact a precondition for the round's central result. **Q12-D6 WITHDRAWN** — `SHEKYL_ANON_ZONE_STEM_FRACTION` had two claimed tiers and neither holds (Q12-D5 removed the structural one; Q12-D5a made the indifference one conditional on unsettled semantics). Withdrawn rather than re-argued, since proposing a replacement now would repeat the failure the round has caught twice. **Q12-D6a — the measurement that could move it needs a RUNNING NETWORK; premise CORRECTED by Bugbot on #430, and the section itself RESTORED.** The draft said nodes "fall to `x=0` by the floor rule" — **that rule does not exist.** F-8b floors the CONFIGURED CAP (`set_max_out_peers` refuses at start, `change_max_out_public_peers` clamps at runtime), never the ACHIEVED peer count, and nothing switches a node's stem fraction off when it cannot fill the floor. **The gap exposed is worse than the inconsistency reported**: a node configuring 12 but reaching 3 keeps stemming at an actual fluff degree below what the embargo derivation assumes — under-provisioned in the privacy-losing direction, the exact condition F-8b exists to prevent, via a path F-8b does not watch. So the question becomes "does such a rule need to EXIST, and what should it do", recorded for the implementation round rather than answered from prose (Q12-D8). The measurement stands and is more useful under the correction: it reports the ACHIEVED anon outbound peer distribution against `t`, the quantity no existing rule observes. No existing instrument reaches it — everything measured so far has been local and synthetic, while peer discovery is a POPULATION DYNAMIC (onion addresses propagating through peerlists over time across joining/leaving nodes), and a static graph cannot answer it because the question is whether the graph FORMS AT ALL. Shape: multi-node testnet with a Tor zone, run to peerlist convergence, readout = anon outbound peer count against `t`, reference = F-8b's 12. Three conditions decide whether the number means anything: (1) `t` is the INDEPENDENT VARIABLE, not a fixture constant — a single-`t` run gives one point and no threshold; (2) peerlist propagation is the mechanism under test so it **cannot be seeded** — a full anon peerlist at startup is fixture-cannot-express-the-input in the arm carrying the whole finding; (3) the failure is **asymmetric and self-reinforcing** — a node below the floor contributes no anon traffic so is less useful as a peer, and whether that damps or spirals is what a static model cannot say. **The most expensive measurement the arc has proposed and the only one that cannot be faked.** The section was DELETED by this round's own D5/D6 rewrite while D7 still cited "the Q12-D6a testnet" — a dangling reference caught by an assertion failing rather than by reading, and restored. **Q12-D7 — BUILD THE MECHANISM FIRST; `p` is not a design question.** The whole `p` discussion concerned a mechanism that has never taken a transaction anywhere: coherence does not run, anon arrivals never reach `relay_transactions`, the pool loop passes `public_` as a literal. Order: (1) the txpool zone field — without it the pool loop cannot route by origin and coherence is unreachable; (2) the receive path, so anon arrivals relay at arrival — §89.8's owed item, and what makes coherence EXECUTE; (3) then `p`, shipped at a stated value and read off `/get_stem_tallies` and `get_connections` on the Q12-D6a testnet. Q12-D6's value, Q12-D4a's semantics and the concavity analysis are SHELVED AS BLOCKED ON THE MECHANISM, not on a decision — not resolved, not owed, unanswerable until step 2; filing them that way is what stops the next session reopening them as though a ruling would settle it. **Q12-D8 — the round's lesson.** Every number in this arc that survived came from RUNNING CODE (F-7's 3250 ms, the 48-cell surface, the linkage matcher, the 2.38x family fix, the Pi/x86 ratio) and none reversed; every number that flip-flopped was reasoned about a branch that does not execute (`p`'s semantics, the partition, the concavity, the cancellation — four reversals in two days, all dormant paths). The test is cheap: **does the thing this number describes currently run?** What the round keeps without needing a number: the cancellation as a property to AIM FOR, and the unified rule as the SHAPE. Q12-D3 collapsed the round's middle: Q12-U2 became mechanical and Q12-U3 narrowed to deriving `p_in` plus deciding whether `p_out` is nonzero. Interlock: #427's tripwire (`an_anonymity_classed_entry_arms_nothing_and_leaves_as_forward`) pins the gap in its current broken state and **fails when this round closes it** — intended, not accidental. |
| **Q12-R1…R14** (the Q12-D6a peer-discovery run) | **The run specification for Q12-D6a** — the multi-node testnet measurement of whether the anon peer graph FORMS, registered here at birth (rule 94 §1) as an extension of the already-registered `Q12-` family; `Q12-R*` = run decisions, `Q12-R-W*` = the work items that must land before the fleet exists. No new prefix is minted. | [`Q12_D6A_PEER_DISCOVERY_RUN.md`](Q12_D6A_PEER_DISCOVERY_RUN.md) | Registered 2026-08-11. Ground findings verified at source and against the live seed estate at pin `14c2ee599`. | **RUN — the full `{15, 30, 60}` sweep (§§13-15) and the Q12-R5 late-joiner control (§15.2) landed 2026-08-14.** **Sweep: 52.1 % / 84.2 % / 85.2 %** of settled node-samples at or above the outbound floor of 12, and **0 nodes never reaching it at any `A`** — so `A` changes how RELIABLY the floor is held, not whether it is attainable. The transition sits between 15 and 30 and is **saturated by 30** (30 -> 60 buys 1.1 points; 15 -> 30 buys 32), which makes the operational launch figure **~30 anonymity-capable nodes** rather than §11.13's arithmetic `A ~ 19` or its "unreachable" phrasing. **Q12-R5: both late-joiner variants reach the floor from a single `--add-peer`, so discovery is NOT hub-dependent** — seed 56 s against ordinary node 247 s, and that ~4.4x IS the price of hub mediation; it is in link establishment, not peerlist acquisition (both held ~30 candidates inside 30 s). Mechanism for the gap left open — the rig records no per-dial outcome. `A = 30` needed no provisioning: it fits the six permanent seed hosts once starts are staggered.  `A = 15`: floor REACHED by all 15 nodes but sustained only 52.1 % of settled samples, against 85.2 % at `A = 60` — so §11.13's "the floor is unreachable up to `A ~ 19`" is directionally right and **too strong in its literal form**; a below-floor rule fires on about half a small network's samples, not all of them. **Two contaminated attempts are committed beside the clean one**: reducing 60 -> 15 left every node holding a 60-address peerlist (two-thirds dead), which read as a plausible 4-8-link small-population result and was caught only by `stored = 60` in a 15-node population; the second attempt raced the wipe against `p2pstate.bin` flush. Standing requirement recorded: an arm that changes `A` starts from verified-empty peerlists, and `stored <= A` is the check. **§16 SETTLES what the floor counts, and corrects the first framing on two counts.** `peer_id` was never a distinctness mechanism on ANY zone — every comparison in `net_node.inl` is self-connection detection (`:1110/:1209/:1234/:2655`, all `is_public`-guarded, the live-connection pair also conjoining `is_same_host`) or peerlist keying; and clearnet has no diversity limit either (`ipv4_network_subnet` appears only in the manual-ban machinery at `:209/:348/:399` — no netgroup bucketing, no per-`/16` cap). **So F-8b and D9 count CONNECTIONS on every zone and always have**; the sentinel made it legible on Tor rather than causing it, which files it against **F-8b's startup refusal** as much as the below-floor rule. **Severity inverts once the adversary's position is priced both ways**: below-floor D9(b) fluff-at-origin gives an adversary holding ANY connection precision ~1 deterministically, against `k/12` and origin/relay-ambiguous if the victim stems — so an adversary WANTS the floor to read low, and moving it low is free (occupy slots, be unreachable, or wait: the sweep says below-floor 47.9 % of the time at `A = 15`, 15.8 % at `A = 30`). Address-counting makes the floor read too HIGH, which denies the adversary the better branch; under full eclipse the check changes nothing either way. Prompt-vs-late (§12.1's counter) is a distribution question SHELVED on the mechanism with the instrument now existing — `converged_fluff_return_mixed` takes degree distributions and refuses unconverged draws. Distinctness mechanisms rejected on the merits: the persona serving credential (`OnionIdentity::from_hs_id_seed`, under StakeEngine custody since #464) would make p2p membership paid, give relay peers persistent identity and correlate relay position with archival service — a privacy regression wearing a sybil-resistance argument; per-zone random `peer_id`s catch only the accidental case and are worse than nothing because they read as a defense. **No code change required**; prose corrected at 3 sites to say *connections*, and §12.2's D9 input is to be a NAMED TYPE (`plan_relay_with_refresh` binds `read_ids(outbound, n)` to a local called `peers` while holding connection ids — an earlier "it already receives the number" estimate is retracted). The floor is a self-provisioning check against configuration and churn, **not a sybil defense, and never scoped as one**. **§17 (PR #476, merged e0d987071) ran §16.4's pre-registered gate and OVERTURNED D9(b)'s implementation** — achieved α(11) = 0.884 against a 0.5 boundary (supremum of α* over the corrected admissibility region `E + R ≤ L`), with the sharper mode firing too: no threshold in the measurable range makes fluff-at-origin beneficial, so the *mechanism* was wrong, not its number; §17.6 then showed the floor gates OUT-degree while the embargo runs on IN-degree (fleet `r(out,in)` = +0.050, below-floor in-degree deficit 0.30 links), killing §12.1's premise by a shorter route. **§18 re-rules D9 (2026-08-15): STEM ANYWAY — the floor never selects wire behavior.** Dominance: origin/relay ambiguity survives interception at any `k/d`, while fluff-at-origin surrenders it to every attacker link at precision ≈ 1; *hold* rejected on three grounds including adversary-triggerable censorship. `d = 0` send-nothing is §30.5 / `zone_route` usability, not a D9 exception. There is no below-floor *check*; the achieved count is an **admin-only observer** (stem-tallies snapshot + transition log, never `get_status()`, never the public RPC surface) under the §18.3 principle: **gate on what the operator controls (config refusal stands), only observe what the adversary can influence (the floor never selects wire behavior)**. Degree-adaptive embargo refused in advance (embargo length is wire-measurable, so it would write the victim's count onto the wire). The `fluff_return_ms` concurrency bar is discharged by construction — §18 moves no conformance vector, so the §90.4 landing decision proceeds independently. Implementation owed (Rust, rule 20): transition logging + admin-surface field + named count type whose floor comparison is a constructor, not `Ord`. Arm result: 60 anon + 15 clearnet detector over 9 hosts / 9 regions / 3 providers, one binary (`sha256 18984a08…`), every non-seed bootstrapped from **exactly one** seed onion. **The graph forms** — 85.2 % of node-samples at or above the outbound floor of 12 over 12 samples, **0/60 never reaching it** and every node's max at 12-13, and the one node observed at a single link in the settling sample left that state by the next. Isolation control passed (`END 15 0`, all clearnet at 0 anon links). Caveat recorded at the number: 12 is the configured `tx-proxy` cap, so nodes at 12 are **saturated, not clear** — the arm shows the floor reachable, not exceeded, and it does **not** test §11.13's launch condition, which lives at small `A`. Readout committed at [`data/q12-d6a-a60-2026-08-14/series.tsv`](data/q12-d6a-a60-2026-08-14/series.tsv); driver at `utils/fleet/run_arm.sh`. **Q12-R14 registered here (rule 94 §1): daemon startup peaks at ~2× steady-state RSS** (274 MB settled, killed at 520 MB), so simultaneous starts OOM one instance per 8 GB host and silently shrink `A`, the independent variable — starts are staggered and each phase verifies `alive == want`. Also in-round: §9.1's `ARCH=native` AVX-512 trap reproduced itself the moment a build was made outside the recorded recipe (six of nine hosts lack AVX-512), so the portable flags now live in the container recipe rather than in prose. Original ground findings: (a) the request to put the seeds' `.onion` addresses into the peerlist they PUSH **cannot work and would ban the seeds** — `handle_remote_peerlist` (`net_node.inl:2109-2117`) rejects a whole peerlist containing any cross-zone entry, and both callers respond with `add_host_fail` plus a connection close (`:1072-1077` handshake, `:1152-1157` timed-sync), so a seed appending its onion over clearnet accumulates failure counts at every bootstrapping node until it is banned; there is no seed exception and adding one would be wrong, because a cross-zone entry IS the IP↔onion deanonymization link and the check is receiver-side and unconditional. (b) The intent is served by two OTHER mechanisms: the compiled per-zone `get_seed_nodes(zone::tor)` list for hop one, and ordinary self-announce on outgoing anon links (`:2637-2643`) thereafter. (c) **Rule-60 defect found: the compiled tor and i2p seed lists are still MONERO's** — six `plowsof*`/`zbjkbsxc*` `.onion` addresses on Monero's port 18083 (`:762-769`) and three Monero `.b32.i2p` (`:775-779`); the IPv4 list is correctly Shekyl's. A Shekyl mainnet node with `--tx-proxy tor` today bootstraps its anon zone against Monero. (d) **`--add-peer` routes by parsed zone** (`:845-846`), so anon bootstrap needs NO code change — the fleet unlock; `--seed-node` by contrast feeds `public_zone` only (`:527-533`). (e) **The seed estate is not configured correctly**: all four binaries date 2026-03-30 and predate the entire relay arc (stale-daemon oracle), `skl-seedusw` is DOWN on the pristine example conf, and **no tor process runs anywhere so no onion service exists yet**; `skl-seeduse`'s restricted/unrestricted RPC split is CORRECT for its `shekyl-web` role, not a defect. Decisions: **Q12-R1** onions go in the compiled per-zone seed list, not the pushed peerlist; **Q12-R2** the run builds from current `dev` and bootstraps purely via `--add-peer`, and the compiled TESTNET list lands AFTER the runs — compiling it first gives every fleet binary four anon seeds no CLI can suppress, which would contaminate the late-joiner control; the MAINNET Monero deletion is separable and lands immediately; **Q12-R3** clearnet-only nodes STAY in the fleet as the isolation detector for the propagation arm (colocation is safe: `is_same_host` at `:1202` is ANDed with `peer_id` equality, so colocated nodes are distinct peers); **Q12-R4** `A` (absolute anon-capable count) is the independent variable and `t` is derived, swept `A ∈ {15,30,60}` = 80/40/20 % of F-8b's floor of 12; **Q12-R5** the bootstrap control is a LATE JOINER with exactly one `--add-peer` (isolates gossip from bootstrap in one run; a seed-count sweep conflates them); **Q12-R6** one tor process per node with its own `DataDirectory` (SPIKE-F-12: guard sets are per-process), but multiple nodes per VM is fine — the underlying IP is invisible to every mechanism under test. **Amends Q12-D6a condition (2)**: "peerlists cannot be seeded" taken literally makes the run impossible, since anon discovery has no out-of-band bootstrap; the correct form is **seed ADDRESSES only, never a populated peerlist**. Work before the fleet: **Q12-R-W1** repo PR (delete the Monero seed lists) and **Q12-R-W2** rebuild the estate from `dev`, restart `seedusw`, pinned Tor Expert Bundle + one HS per seed with **keys backed up off-host before the addresses are compiled into source**, cross-`--add-peer` the four onions. **Q12-R-W3** guard the anon-zone `peer_id` sentinel — INDEPENDENT of the run and can ride W1's PR, both touching `add_zone`'s neighbourhood. Checked and answered: `peer_id` CANNOT correlate a Tor peer to a clearnet IP today, because it is per-zone (`m_config` is a `network_zone` member) and only the public zone is randomized (`net_node.inl:147`); `add_zone` never touches `m_config`, so an anon zone keeps `config_t`'s ctor default of 1 (`net_node.h:157`) and every node announces `peer_id = 1` on its Tor zone — uniform, zero entropy. Every comparator is gated (`:1097`/`:2691` on `azone == public_`, `:1196`/`:1221` on `is_public`, which is why `:1202`'s `is_same_host` never evaluates on an anon connection). **The wire surface is THREE emitters, not two**: `:2130` handshake, `:2641` anon self-announce, and `:2767` `handle_ping` — the third ungated and zone-dynamic, correct today and correct under any per-zone scheme, which is what makes it invisible; it emits the sentinel because the sentinel is what is there, not because anything checks. **The safety is a DEFAULT, not an invariant**: moving the `crypto::rand` call into `add_zone` as an apparent cleanup gives every node a stable unique id announced on BOTH its clearnet and Tor connections, making onion→IP a passive lookup with no timing analysis. **Guard placement is not the obvious site** — a check at the TOP of `add_zone` runs BEFORE the feared edit's `crypto::rand` and sails past the very edit it exists to catch; the invariant belongs downstream of every construction path, fail-closed after zone setup in `init()`, with a named `ANON_ZONE_SENTINEL_PEER_ID` carrying the reason at its definition and a gtest on the announced value (legitimate on the config value only because all three emitters are direct unmodified member reads). **RETRACTION 2026-08-11**: W1 also claimed `--add-peer <x>.onion` with no tor zone throws uncaught `std::out_of_range` at `:846`. **It does not** — `handle_command_line` calls `add_zone(adr->get_zone())` at `:494` before pushing to `m_command_line_peers` (and `:2869` likewise), so the zone always exists; and the diagnostic the claim asked for ALREADY EXISTS at `:638-645` ("Set outgoing peer for tor but did not set --tx-proxy"). Written from the call site without reading the callers that populate the map — Q12-D8's test applied to a control-flow assumption rather than a number. The owed placement check is DISCHARGED in the same pass: all four `add_zone` callers (`:494`, `:615`, `:654`, `:2869`) are reached from `handle_command_line`, which `init()` calls first, so no zone is constructed post-`init()` and the invariant sits after `init_config()`. **BUILT on `fix/p2p-anon-zone-hygiene`**, both legs negative-controlled by injecting the feared edit: defect alone → `init` refuses, logging the correlation as the reason; defect with the guard DELETED → the announced-value assertion fails and prints the leaked identifier `15317140264242224297`, so the test catches it independently of the guard. The test's own negative control asserts the public zone does NOT read as the sentinel. Full unit suite 1055 passed, 0 failed. **Q12-R10 RULED — THE FLEET MUST BE NETWORK-ISOLATED; this is a BLOCKER on the run.** The density test showed a fleet node holding an outbound CLEARNET peer: `get_ip_seed_nodes` returns the four production seeds for testnet, so fleet nodes join the LIVE testnet automatically. First written up as consistent with Q12-R3 — **that was wrong**: R3 ruled the seeds out of the HISTOGRAM, whereas this is a path to an unbounded, unmeasured population OUTSIDE the fleet. **It breaks `A` as the independent variable** — at A=15 a node's anonymity peers may come from its 14 fleet peers or from however many Tor-capable nodes the live testnet has, so the floor could be reached ENTIRELY FROM OUTSIDE the fleet and the histogram would report a healthy 12 unrelated to A. The contamination is **inversely correlated with the variable** (worst where the fleet is smallest — the arm the finding rests on) and **cannot be corrected afterwards**, since a converged histogram does not record whether a peer was fleet- or testnet-sourced. The counter-argument that a real network is not sealed either is true and beside the point: under it the run measures the testnet's Tor population with a fleet attached, and the sweep is meaningless. **Ruled: a fleet build with a distinct `NETWORK_ID` AND empty compiled seed lists.** Verified at source that `--seed-node` alone is INSUFFICIENT — `net_node.inl:1749-1759` appends `get_ip_seed_nodes()` as a FALLBACK when no configured seed connects, so production seeds re-enter by a path overriding the seed list does not close; `--add-exclusive-node` REJECTED because it changes peer selection, the mechanism under test; firewalling alone REJECTED because the daemon still dials and fails, burning Q12-R8 backoff on the measured connections. Emptying the lists as well as changing `NETWORK_ID` is not redundant for that same reason. **The oracle is argued, not assumed**: the fleet binary differs only in `NETWORK_ID` and seed constants, `NETWORK_ID` gates HANDSHAKE ACCEPTANCE rather than discovery, and the discovery path (gossip, zone segregation, self-announce, maintainer + backoff) is byte-identical. **Q12-R11 RULED — at A <= 12 the floor is UNREACHABLE BY ARITHMETIC.** 10 anonymity nodes, floor 12, 9 possible peers, no node above 8 outbound: `12 > A-1` means EVERY node sits below the floor, not because discovery failed but because the population is smaller than the requirement. **This makes Q12-U2's open question the LAUNCH CONDITION rather than an edge case** — whatever a below-floor node should do (not stem / stem anyway / hold) is what EVERY node does while the anonymity population is under 13, which is what a young network IS. Q12-D6a recorded that no such rule exists and that F-8b floors the CONFIGURED CAP rather than the achieved count; R11 bounds when that gap is load-bearing, and it is exactly at launch. **It also narrows the A=15 arm**, which asks whether NEAR-COMPLETE CONNECTIVITY forms (12 of 14 possible peers) rather than whether selection is selective — it must not be read as the low end of a trend; only A=60 makes the floor a genuine selection among candidates. **Density VERIFIED on one host** (4 vCPU/8 GB): 10 daemons + 10 tor, 0 restarts, 3.6/7.9 GB, all ports bound, 10 distinct guard dirs; the `.deb` supplies a BINARY, NOT A TOPOLOGY (its unit is single-instance and hardcodes data-dir/log/StateDirectory/ReadWritePaths with no `--config-file`), so the fleet needs `skl-node@`/`skl-tor@` templates with `Requires=skl-tor@%i` making R8's ordering STRUCTURAL. **Q12-R8's two-reading rule earned itself on first use** — 29 outbound at t+0 against 62 at t+240, so a single reading would have reported under half the converged count. **Q12-R8 RULED — the arms need a defined start ordering and settle time; CONTROLLED RESULT, not an explanation after the fact.** A node down when its peers dial is not dialled again for a long time: the anonymity zone's connection maintainer backs off and the backoff outlives the outage. Observed (seedaus at 0 tor peers after a rolling restart while seedeu/seeduse paired only with each other), predicted, then REPRODUCED BY RESTORING THE CONDITION — restarting seedaus alone once peers were live gave 2 peers immediately, and a second rolling restart with all five tor daemons + hidden services up beforehand produced a FULL MESH on the first pass (3-4 peers on all five). The effect appears when the condition holds and disappears when removed. **Threatens the run directly**: at A=60 a rolling start guarantees early nodes dial peers that do not exist yet, and the backoff then suppresses the ACHIEVED anonymity peer count — the exact quantity measured — for a reason unrelated to peer discovery, presenting as a low x against A, which IS the run's headline finding. Order: all tor + HS up first, then daemons, then settle, then **two readings separated by longer than the backoff**; an arm whose readings disagree is NOT reported as a measurement. The discriminator matters more than the ordering — without it a slower fleet reproduces the artifact and reports it as adoption sensitivity. **Q12-R9 RULED + FIXED — a destructive move guarded by a predicate that could not see its own subject.** The deploy script used `[ -d .../lmdb ]` where the intent was a SCHEMA VERSION; true whenever a daemon has ever run, so the second pass moved aside the freshly built CURRENT database (nil cost at height 1; on a synced chain it deletes the chain). **The more useful half is the backup**: safety rested entirely on the `.bak` suffix and the same script's `rm -rf` overwrote it, so **reversibility was never a property of the operation — only of running it exactly once**, and the second invocation destroyed the recovery path silently. **Third instance of one class in this arc**: `\| tail` discarding the artifact and swallowing an exit code, `;` making a gate decorative, and now a `.bak` that survives one run — all look defensive, none is, none fails loudly. Fixed as `utils/fleet/check_chain_db_compatible.sh` — **a CLASSIFIER that touches nothing**. It began as a retirement tool and FOUR review rounds found FOUR defects, all one mistake: the database was located from a `data-dir` ARGUMENT while compatibility was judged from a CONFIG with its own `data-dir=`; a probe timeout was read as compatible; `find` on an unreadable directory returns nothing; and `[ ! -e ]` is false when a PARENT is unsearchable, reporting a hidden database as a fresh node. The first is a wrong vantage point and the rest are **the caller inferring, from its own uid/cwd/permissions, the state of a tree the daemon opens from its own** — the caller's silence repeatedly taken for the daemon's absence. A fifth guard would have been a fifth chance at the same mistake, so the VANTAGE POINT WAS REMOVED: no filesystem introspection, no destructive action, one question (*will this daemon start with this config?*) answered as 0 usable / 10 incompatible / 2 refused, with the caller deciding in the open where a `rm -rf` is visible rather than hidden behind a `.bak` that proved reversible exactly once. 141 lines -> 97. The untestable retire path is not untested but GONE. It asks the DAEMON for the verdict rather than parsing the schema (a second version check would be a duplicate free to drift from the one that actually refuses), REFUSES ON UNKNOWN, and never overwrites a backup (timestamped destination, `mv -n`, plus a post-move assertion turning a no-op move into a failure). Fixing the predicate alone would NOT have saved the backups — an independent defect on the same line. Verified across no-database / current-schema-left-untouched / corrupt-refused-with-zero-backups, and the matcher against the real captured pre-V9 text, a future pre-V10, and an unrelated MDB_CORRUPTED; **the retire path itself is NOT exercised end-to-end and the doc says so** — producing a genuine pre-V9 database is no longer possible because R9's own defect destroyed the last copies. **Q12-R-W2 COMPLETE**: five hosts (skl-seedjp added, Debian 13 vs the others' Ubuntu 24.04), one binary, five hidden services, meshed at 3-4 anonymity peers each, keys backed up and sha256-verified. **Q12-R7 RULED — build at a portable baseline, not at the build host.** The first Ubuntu 24.04 artifact ran on three seeds and died with `Illegal instruction` on `skl-seedaus`, a DigitalOcean host without AVX-512 where the three Vultr Skylake boxes have it and the dev box has more still. `CMakeLists` defaults `ARCH` to `native` (`set_default_arch`), so the container compiled for the machine that was handy — **rule 76's failure reached through a build flag rather than a constant**. Rebuilt with `-DARCH=x86-64` plus `-C target-cpu=x86-64` for the Rust side; verified to emit zero AVX-512 instructions and to run on all hosts. The INTERSECTION of the current hosts would also be wrong — the fleet is arbitrary instance types across three providers, so the floor is generic `x86-64`, not what these hosts happen to share. Relay nodes are not compute-bound and RandomX carries its own runtime dispatch, so the cost is nil. Build target: dev box is Debian 13 (glibc ~2.41), seeds are Ubuntu 24.04 (2.39) — build for 24.04 and pin the fleet image to match. **Banner on every number the run produces**: the testnet can validate MECHANISM but cannot produce representative anonymity numbers (the graph is too small relative to stem length) — a testnet-derived precision figure read as a network property is `peers = 8` again. **The ISOLATION arm has nothing to detect until Q12-U1 + Q12-U2 are written — verified at source 2026-08-11.** An anon-arrived tx is classed `relay_method::forward`; the pool cycle routes `forward` into `stem_req` (`cryptonote_core.cpp:1070-1071`) and relays `stem_req` to `zone::public_` AS A LITERAL (`:1091`) — the only destination that branch has. So anon traffic takes ONE anon hop and crosses to clearnet BY CONSTRUCTION; a clearnet-only node failing to receive is an event the current code cannot produce, and the arm would report a TRIVIAL PASS. Stated at the spec rather than discovered after, because a green isolation result is exactly what gets cited later as evidence the zones bridge — Q12-D8's test applied BEFORE the measurement. **This is sequencing, not deferral (rule 22)**: U1/U2 are code this arc owns and is next to build, ordered by Q12-D7, with nothing external gating them. Writing them is what gives the detector something to detect. The clearnet-only set stays in the fleet, instrumented and unread until then. Discovery runs FIRST — independent of U1/U2, pure peerlist gossip with zero chain activity, and its result could invalidate the anon-stem design outright, the cheapest thing to learn early; the isolation arm additionally needs mining, maturity and funded wallets (chain is at height 1 / difficulty 1). **Q12-R5 amended**: the late joiner's single `--add-peer` must be a NON-SEED fleet node, and both variants run — pointed at a seed it measures gossip-from-hub (a seed knowing 12 is not the graph gossiping), pointed at an ordinary node it measures gossip proper, and the DIFFERENCE prices how much of discovery is hub-mediated. **Q12-R-W1 REFRAMED — the Monero seed lists are a GENESIS BLOCKER, not hygiene**: `get_seed_nodes(zone::tor)` returned Monero's onions on Monero's port gated on MAINNET, so at genesis a Shekyl node with `--tx-proxy tor` dials six Monero hidden services, fails the network-ID handshake at each, and has nowhere else to go — the Tor zone never forms and Tor is NON-FUNCTIONAL ON MAINNET AT LAUNCH — while announcing Shekyl's Tor population to another network's seed operators on the way. Empty is not a resting state either: until Shekyl's onions land in `get_seed_nodes` (Q12-R1) the zone has no default bootstrap path, `--add-peer` being a manual step. LANDED on `fix/p2p-anon-zone-hygiene` (PR #440) with Q12-R-W3. **Q12-R12 RULED — THE ISOLATED NETWORK ALREADY EXISTS; NO FLEET BUILD IS MADE. Supersedes Q12-R10's REMEDY, not its requirement.** R10 demanded a distinct `NETWORK_ID` and no compiled seed dial targets, then specified a modified build to obtain them; `--stagenet` already has BOTH in the shipped binary. Verified at source: `::config::stagenet::NETWORK_ID` (`cryptonote_config.h:429-431`) shares no bytes with testnet's at `:418-420`; `get_ip_seed_nodes()`'s STAGENET branch is EMPTY (`net_node.inl:736-739`) with the four production seeds inside the TESTNET branch; `get_seed_nodes()` returns `{}` for tor/i2p on EVERY network (`:770-772`). `stagenet_hard_forks[]` is `{1,1,0,...}` byte-identical to testnet's (`hardforks.cpp:41-50`), so stagenet is v3-from-genesis with every feature active — it differs in `NETWORK_ID`, genesis tx, ports (13021/13029) and address prefix, and in NOTHING peer discovery touches. Verified empirically: stock `shekyld --stagenet` starts on a fresh data-dir, creates genesis, and DIALS NOTHING (no `134.199.*`/`45.7*` in its log — the seed set is empty rather than overridden). **This discharges §11.1's oracle paragraph BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by argument** — the fleet runs the same artifact a user installs, so the "a modified binary is a different oracle unless the difference is argued" reasoning is not needed at all; and it deletes a build (no depends/gitian pass, no second `.deb`, no drift between fleet and release artifacts). Four consequences: (1) the PRODUCTION seeds run testnet and CANNOT participate, so each seed host runs a SECOND stagenet daemon with its own tor process, `DataDirectory` and hidden service (SPIKE-F-12) — §5's rule is unchanged, seeds are bootstrap targets and gossip participants and are NOT in the histogram; (2) the fleet's seed onions stay OUT OF SOURCE (passed as `--add-peer` at provisioning), so Q12-R1's "HS keys are infrastructure once compiled into source" does NOT attach — they are disposable fixtures with no backup obligation, unlike the testnet seed keys; (3) bootstrap MUST use `--add-peer`, not `--seed-node`, verified at `net_node.inl:527-533` where `--seed-node` parses into `public_zone.m_seed_nodes` ONLY and cannot carry an onion into the anonymity zone, whereas `--add-peer` routes by parsed zone (`add_zone(adr->get_zone())` at `:494`); (4) ports change to 13021/13029 and every template/config/firewall rule written against 12021/12029 must be swept — a hardcoded testnet port is the one way a fleet node could still reach the live network. **Q12-R13 RULED — THE BACKOFF IS ONE HOUR, AND Q12-R8's ORDERING RULE WAS TOO WEAK.** R8 said "two readings separated by longer than the backoff" without knowing the backoff; it is **3600 s**, so the density test's 240 s separation was short by 15x. A three-node stagenet smoke test on the dev box (three daemons, three tor processes, three v3 hidden services, chained A<-B<-C so C must learn A by GOSSIP) sat at ZERO connections with ONE dial attempt in the log — `Timeout on socks connect` at t+47s, then nothing for an hour. At source: `record_addr_failed` writes `m_conn_fails_cache[addr.host_str()] = now` (`net_node.inl:1404-1408`), `is_addr_recently_failed` returns true until `now - t > P2P_FAILED_ADDR_FORGET_SECONDS` (`:1411-1421`), the constant is `(60*60)` (`cryptonote_config.h:201`), and it bites at white/gray selection `:1439`, the maintainer candidate loop `:1698` and peerlist append `:2135` — the log line `No available peer in white list filtered by 1` IS this filter. The cache is keyed on `host_str()`, so an ONION is filtered by its full address, with no per-zone exemption and no way to clear it short of a restart. **The first dial failed even though R8's rule was followed exactly** — all three tor processes were at `Bootstrapped 100%` before any daemon started, because a hidden service being PUBLISHED is not the same as its descriptor being FETCHABLE by another client; reaching the same onion by hand through the same SOCKS port minutes later succeeded immediately. So R8 is STRENGTHENED: every onion must be **verified reachable through another node's SOCKS proxy** before any daemon starts, the two readings must be **>3600 s** apart, and a fleet whose first dials failed is RESTARTED rather than waited out. **This is the run's largest scheduling fact**: at A=60 a rolling start means early nodes dial onions not yet fetchable, each failure costs an hour of suppression on the exact quantity being measured, and a histogram read at t+20min would report a sparse graph caused entirely by the start procedure. Found for the price of one dev box, before any paid arm. **CONFIRMED TO WITHIN FOUR SECONDS, no argument left.** The three-node smoke test was left running and polled every 60 s: daemons started 00:58:25Z, `B`'s only dial failed at **00:59:12.622Z**, all three nodes held ZERO connections for the next sixty minutes, and at **01:59:16.106Z** -- 3.5 s after `00:59:12 + 3600` -- `B` connected to `A`, converging by 02:00:26Z to 5 of 6 possible links with `C` reaching `A` BY GOSSIP having never been given its address. **Nothing changed except the clock**: same binaries, same tor processes, same configs, no restart, no intervention. The hour is not a symptom of a broken peer, an unreachable service or a misconfiguration -- it is the daemon declining to retry. **The gate found a SECOND failure mode on first use**: one of the three hidden services was UNREACHABLE from both other nodes more than an hour after `Bootstrapped 100%`, while answering from its OWN tor (which proves only that the local listener is up, since tor short-circuits a request for a service it hosts) — so the gate is a HEALTH CHECK, not a settling delay, and a node whose onion never becomes fetchable enters the histogram as an isolated node, biasing the statistic IN THE DIRECTION OF THE RUN'S HEADLINE CLAIM; such nodes are restarted or dropped before the arm and the drop count is reported. The `>3600 s` separation is NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT — a node that burned addresses at t+0 expires them at t+3600 and must then RETRY AND CONVERGE, so the rule is THREE readings (or one well after) rather than two with a bigger gap. **Q12-R13 IS A DAEMON DEFECT, NOT A RUN PRECONDITION — the mainnet bootstrap spiral.** Run the same sequence with a real new user: a node starts with `--tx-proxy tor`, dials the compiled onion seeds through freshly built circuits, the descriptors are PUBLISHED BUT NOT YET FETCHABLE, every dial fails, every seed onion is suppressed for an hour with no retry and no clearing path, the node has no anon peers, cannot self-announce, receives no inbound — **and on restart it does the same thing**, because the state that would make the second attempt succeed is tor's while the state that suppresses it is the daemon's. This is **Q12-D6a's spiral reached by a mechanism the design did not predict**: not "no outgoing connection => no self-announcement", but "one badly-timed dial round costs an hour, and the failure is at the DESCRIPTOR layer rather than the peer's" — the peer is up, correct, and unreachable for a reason having nothing to do with it. **The constant is IP-era and its justification does not survive the transport change**: 3600 s is reasonable for an IPv4 address (a failed dial usually means a down host) and wrong for an onion (a failed dial is frequently a transient descriptor fetch against a host that is up) — the same shape as `FORWARD_DELAY` and `peers = 8` (rule 16), a constant carried across a boundary its derivation never crossed. **RULED: the suppression window is a property of the TRANSPORT and the anon-zone value is DERIVED, not picked** — `is_addr_recently_failed` consults a PER-ZONE window (public keeps 3600 s, its justification intact); the anonymity-zone window is provisioned from the DESCRIPTOR-FETCH LATENCY DISTRIBUTION, which this run measures directly since `wait_onions_reachable.sh` times every one of 60 onions from 60 tor processes (**the run gains a deliverable it was not built for**); and repeated failures escalate so that a FIRST failure never costs an hour. The constant lands AFTER the arms — a named dependency, not a deferral (rule 22): the measurement supplying the input is scheduled, and the finding is registered now so the value cannot be quietly picked. **Consequence for Q12-D9 (§12 amended)**: `x = 0` was chosen because the operator's exposure is BOUNDED BY RECOVERY, and under R13 a node that fell below the floor through failed dials cannot promptly climb back — the peers it would recover with are precisely the ones it just burned. The below-floor state is STICKIER than §12.1 priced, and on a young network it compounds with Q12-R11 (population too small to reach the floor, retry path suppressed). The ruling STANDS — the alternatives are worse under stickiness, not better — but the accepted cost is larger than stated and the live check must expect a peer count that cannot recover promptly. **RULED AND FIXED 2026-08-12 — PR #451 MERGED** (`7c61b7d63`), after landing with the design record in #448. **VERIFIED IN THE ARTIFACT, not just in the source**: the fleet binary rebuilt from that merge (sha `0f1a4f6b…`) was pointed at an onion nothing serves and logged its own retry ladder at **243 / 483 / 961 s** — the 240 s window doubling to 480 and 960, each gap carrying ~3 s for the dial itself. Under the unfixed constant there is one attempt and then silence for an hour. That check exists because "the source tree contained the fix" is not the same claim as "this binary carries it", and this arc was already bitten once by a daemon that predated the commit it was supposed to have. The two constants have DIFFERENT derivations, and treating them as one was the coupling error applied to the fix: the timeout answers "how long before concluding this dial failed", the suppression answers "how long before retrying", which contains no latency at all. **The timeout does NOT change.** 60 cold descriptor fetches probed at a 120 s cap (so the distribution is observed rather than censored at the constant under test) are BIMODAL: every failure sat at the cap between 119.2 and 119.7 s with one success between at 78.3 s, so a fetch resolves under ~30 s or not at all and EVERY timeout from 30 to 75 s gives an identical failure rate. The measurement's value is NEGATIVE — it kills the plausible wrong fix (raise the timeout) before a round is spent on it — and n=60 justifies no reduction either. **The suppression is derived from COST.** `m_connections_maker_interval` is `once_a_time_seconds<1>` and `connections_maker` loops until the target is met or candidates run out, so a dozen candidates are fully attempted within minutes against a suppression measured in hours; the burned set is established almost at once. A node reaches F-8b's floor only if at most `A-1-12` candidates burn — about a third of the time at A=15 — so **below roughly A=19 the floor is unreachable BY SUPPRESSION**, peers present and answering, node having blacklisted them. That WIDENS Q12-R11 from A<=12-by-arithmetic to A<=19-by-suppression, a band half again as wide for a different reason, and composed with Q12-D9 it means **on a young network NO SHEKYL NODE ANYWHERE STEMS OVER TOR** with no indication to the operator — the launch condition, and why the fix is pre-genesis. **The restart loop, every step measured**: ~1 node in 5 fails to publish while reporting healthy; the documented remedy is restarting tor; the restart republishes with NEW intro points; dials in that window fail at **55%** against 12.7% settled (pooled 38/300, 95% CI 9.4-16.9%); every dialler during it is blind for an hour, so the node looks broken and the operator restarts again. **The repair sustains the failure.** **CONFIRMED TWICE**: a local three-node test held ZERO connections for sixty minutes and connected 3.5 s after `t_fail + 3600` with nothing changed but the clock; the six-host ring had SEVEN log timeouts and EXACTLY seven missing links, all expiring inside an 88-second window, reading a complete 30/30 mesh at the next sample. The 21-sample hour also characterises steady-state churn — 30/30 held with one ±1 transient recovering inside one 3-minute interval — so an ordinary loss recovers in minutes where a burn is gone for an hour: indistinguishable in a fleet total, obvious in a per-node series, which is why **the arms record PER-NODE TIME SERIES**. **THE FIX (PR #451, merged)**: `P2P_ANON_FAILED_ADDR_FORGET_SECONDS = 240`, the **p90** of measured post-restart recovery (20 services restarted on the same keys: 20/20 recovered, median 96 s, p90 239 s, max 262 s), doubling per consecutive failure to the UNCHANGED public-zone hour and **cleared by a successful handshake** — without which a transiently flaky peer ratchets to the ceiling and reaches the same disconnection by a slower road. The asymmetry forces a quantile: too SHORT retries into the same dead interval, fails, escalates, pushes the next attempt out, so under-estimating COMPOUNDS while over-estimating costs a slightly slower first retry (F's shape, hop's missing one). p90 is also the largest quantile the retry budget admits — three attempts over twelve candidates cost 3300 s at 240 s against 3720 s at the observed maximum, which would exceed the hour being avoided. **The quantile is NAMED AT THE CONSTANT** so a later reader does not correct a tail figure toward a central one. Cache extracted to `nodetool::failed_addr_cache` (owns map + schedule, takes `now` as a parameter) because `record_addr_success` is private on `node_server` and an untested reset is the half that regresses silently; the window derives from `network_address::get_zone()` so NO call site changes. 1067 tests / 1060 passed / 0 failed, **negative-controlled both ways** (reset made a no-op reds exactly one test; per-zone selection removed reds three), time-to-floor asserted in the tests rather than left in prose. **Failures do NOT correlate through the dialler** — four settled rounds, 240 attempts, 30 failures, Wald-Wolfowitz runs test per round (z = +0.45/+0.88/-1.04/+0.24, Stouffer +0.27, p=0.79): dispersed, so the binomial is the right model and p^3 compounding holds. The test carries its own POSITIVE CONTROL: on the unsettled condition it returns z=-5.30, p<0.001, because publication progress makes failures decline 94%/83%/14% by start-time third — a SELF-INFLICTED confound, since that condition was chosen FOR power and imported the very trend the test was meant to detect. **A powered test of the wrong quantity is worse than an underpowered one**, because it yields a confident number. **Methodology recorded as findings**: reading the latency experiment at 48 of 60 showed 0 failures where the finished run showed 13.3% (fast attempts finish first, so ANY partial read of a duration experiment is biased fast; countermeasure = refuse-below-expected-count, the fourth refuse-don't-clamp of the arc); and the rate moved 13.3% (n=60) -> 9.2% (n=120) -> 12.7% (n=300), each intermediate figure published as though it were THE estimate and the middle one presented as a correction while being a second draw. **GENERAL FORM: when a measured rate feeds a decision, carry the INTERVAL through the decision rather than picking a value and defending it** — which is why the reachability table is evaluated at 0.10/0.23/0.35 and no single estimate is load-bearing. |

Standalone, wired to nothing. **RP-2** = FFI + `connection_map` cut; **RP-3** = epoch/fluff scheduler cut (`levin_notify.cpp` → transport shim); **RP-4** = `tx_pool.cpp` embargo. History and UPDATE chain follow. Landed 2026-07-23 (measurement + design in one PR). Four defects, all asserted in code: **F-1** the 39 s embargo does not follow from its own stated derivation (paper formula over `k=5, ep=0.10, hop=175ms` gives 16.61 s; 39 s reproduces only under `log10` for `ln`); **F-2** the embargo draws a Poisson while its derivation assumes an *exponential* survival function — measured, the backstop **never fires** (full-travel 1.0000 at both 39 s and a corrected 17 s, against a stem completing in ~700 ms); **F-3** the closed form substitutes `E[K]` into an expression in `K(K-1)` and stem length is geometric (`E[K(K-1)] = 2·E[K](E[K]-1)`), so it under-provisions at every `q` — exact solve gives **144 s** (RD-1 return-term + RD-4 stem-support corrections; cross-checked at 147 s); the pre-RD-1 31 s was a loose lower bound; **F-4** the **fluff delay** carries the same distribution defect on the timer every node applies to every tx (Bitcoin Core's cited `PoissonNextSend` is an exponential inter-arrival, not `std::poisson_distribution`) — measured ~1.96× more invertible, and 93% invertible for a tx arriving late in a flush window; **F-5** the same defect makes the fluff flood ~7× slower (13.75 s vs 2.25 s p90 first passage) — the flood-return term that lifts the RD-1 embargo to 112 s, before RD-4's stem-support fix takes it to the adopted **144 s** (F-3). F-2/F-4 are the high-severity pair; correcting F-1 alone changes nothing measurable. Relay timing is node-local policy, **not consensus**: no fork, not genesis-blocking, but being fixed pre-ship because it is wrong. | RP-1 landed (crate + design Round 2: RD-4 stem-support fix → 144 s, black-hole + first-spy instruments, §11 source-anchoring, §6 adversary-position + quantified clearnet↔Tor delta [supernode π₀ 0.45→0.000] + measured clearnet passive channel [mean-dependent, 0.011@144s, zero on Tor]); ≤0.3% worst-case exposure reachable via reshape [2.24%→0.026% at 1 retry, no embargo change] not embargo-lengthening [needs 1050s = liveness break]; §15 block-time seam (embargo held block-time-unaware, reconciled at the integration layer); crate decomposed pre-RP-2 (measurement surface split into focused modules).** RP-2 split into **RP-2a** (faithful `connection_map` port to Rust — logic to `stem_map.rs`, `dandelionpp.{h,cpp}` become opaque-handle FFI glue with removal target RP-3, `levin_notify`/gtests unchanged; port anchors re-verified at source. RP-2a **MERGED** (#370, merge `d4bb9b117`; archived `archive/rp2a-connection-map-rust-2026-07-27`): connection_map logic ported to Rust `StemMap` via `shekyl-ffi`; C++ is now an opaque-handle forwarding wrapper (removal target RP-3). Six review rounds. **RP-3 in progress** on `feat/rp3-levin-scheduler-rust` (§18 design round): the scheduler cut — the loop and the zone state move to Rust, closing **F-4/F-5** (the fluff-delay distribution defect and the ~7× slower flood). Re-census correction: the 33 `levin_notify` gtests **do** survive the cut — they force each step via the `run_epoch`/`run_stems`/`run_fluff` hooks (36 calls, zero timer manipulation), so the oracle is timing-independent by construction. Proposed 3a (Dandelion++ path, findings close here) / 3b (noise channels). **RP-3a built** (branch green: 33/33 `levin_notify`, full unit suite 1011 passed / 0 failed): `levin_notify.cpp` is a transport shim over `shekyl-relay::{Zone, Driver}` — `contexts`/`map`/`connection_count`/`fluffing` are Rust-owned behind one handle, two asio timers collapse to one armed from `next_wake()` (Rust owns the deadline value, asio owns the sleep — no reactor added, so the crate's reason-2 seal holds and the earlier seal-break was **withdrawn** as mispriced: F-4/F-5 close without it). Two regressions the port introduced and the round caught: the i2p/tor **outbound-only fluff** rule was dropped (caught by the 8 `private_*` gtests — an inbound peer on a hidden service is a stranger who dialled us), and the **epoch rollover merged instead of rebuilding** the stem map, freezing the stem graph and every source's pinning for the life of the process (*not* caught — all 33 gtests passed both before and after the fix, because they assert pinning holds *within* an epoch, which a frozen map satisfies more strongly). **RP-2a seam retired here** on the option §16.1 acceptance item 2 reserved: `src/net/dandelionpp.{h,cpp}`, `dandelionpp_ffi.h`, the `shekyl_dandelionpp_map_*` exports and the 6 `dandelionpp_map` gtests are deleted (five Rust twins verified present in `stem_map.rs`; census by grep). Of the three §16.1 contracts, two are moot and **index-order-with-nils survived** as the `SlotsCb` contract — its successor witness was found sealed-but-empty and was negative-controlled before the deletion landed. **RP-3a MERGED** (#374, merge `0a27d519e`; archived `archive/rp3-levin-scheduler-rust-2026-07-28`). **RP-2b MERGED** (#377, merge `ab8a0987e`; archived `archive/rp2b-churn-stable-alternate-2026-07-28`): each source's stem candidates freeze at first pin, closing the W3c mid-epoch re-roll; the Q-10 ρ obligation shrinks to a single draw **per epoch** (still blocked on the `g_max` grounding). **RP-3b CLOSED 2026-07-29** (§20, branch `feat/rp3b-covert-channels-rust`; **MERGED #380 2026-07-30**): the covert-channel port — per-channel noise timers cut N→**zero** (deadlines Rust-owned, folded into `next_wake()`, zone strand sole FFI caller); the slot-push seam **inverted then deleted** (`SlotsCb`/`update_channel`/`Effect::StemSlots` gone; the binding travels with each `CovertSend`, a slot's loss crosses as `CovertUnbind` derived per due tick — commands return nothing, all effects ride `poll`/force hooks); the RP-3a seal test retired into three named, negative-controlled successors; four invariants witnessed with run-and-observed-to-fail controls (CV-1 repoint-discards-remainder as a gtest sequenced *after* the second discard site died, CV-2 binding identity + hole, CV-3 deadline stability, CV-4 payload-blindness structural — no discriminant in the callback signature); the noise gtests rewritten under the `gtest_dropped_connection` standard after the inherited expectations were shown to assert hook-synchronized emission (the negation of constant-rate cover); `zone::noise` split into `covert_payload` + Rust-owned `covert_enabled` (one birth site); `run_stems` renamed `run_next_wake`. Round outcome in §20.10 records the cost honestly: four unanticipated findings (CV-4 at grounding, the oracle-drive coupling, the synchronization fiction, the `CovertUnbind` dormant-queue leak at the deletion) and **four of ten acceptance criteria wrong as written** (items 1/4/5/8), every one caught at execution rather than review. Remaining in the arc: **Q-11 IN FLIGHT** (Unit 0 landed 2026-07-31, §22 — feasible-region census: `FORWARD_DELAY_*` decoupled from `NOISE_*`, dead epoch mirrors deleted, `NOISE_BYTES`/`NOISE_CHANNELS` admitted to the ledger, the `MAX_FRAGMENTS` capacity constraint restated family-survivingly, the cadence oracle re-aimed at production with controls run-and-failed; Unit 1 = adversary-and-capability next), **Q-12 registered and live** (§22.2 — forward-delay mean underived + Poisson family at `tx_pool.cpp:311`; fired by Unit 0's own decoupling per its reopen criterion (a)), Q-10 selection (blocked on `g_max`). The `force_fluff` pass was discharged clean in #382 (`fluff_via_scheduled_drive`). **Q-11 census corrections (#384): F-7 CLOSED at 190 s** — `fluff_return_ms` had been measured under `EveryPeer` (undirected) while anonymity-zone fluff is outbound-only; re-measured under `OutboundOnly` at degree 12 → **F′ = 3250 ms** *(superseded as a value 2026-08-13 — see §90: 3250 was one seed at 24 trials and is the second-lowest of six draws; the converged reading at the same topology is **3500 ms**, and 3500 is a measured lower bound because uniform-at-the-floor is the conservative topology. Still SHIPPED, because moving `F′` re-baselines the embargo, the wallet timeout and the §44 pins together)*, embargo re-derived **144 → 190 s**, wallet timeout **664 → 874 s** (every 144 s / 664 s / 2250 ms figure earlier in this row is the pre-F-7 value, correct under the old input). **F-8b**: outbound-degree floor 12 (`shekyl_relay_zone_min_provisioned_out_peers`, Rust-owned) enforced at the `--tx-proxy` parser, at `set_max_out_peers` (covers `--out-peers` init), and clamped on the runtime `out_peers` path. **Configuration B deleted**: the covert/noise channel was an origin oracle (precision ~1 toward covert-slot peers); `proxy::noise` removed, `disable_noise` a warn-and-ignore no-op, cover-traffic absence warned at startup; the wire-observer defense is Q-11 Unit 2 (§§25, 29-31, 41). UPDATE 2026-08-04: **R-1 mixed eligibility MERGED (#389)** — `shekyl_relay_zone_divert_relayed_tx`, one 2 %/hop eligibility roll at clearnet entry (§59; dual-stack `select_anonymity` fixes rode along); the anonymity-coherence arm stays dormant while hidden-service zones force fluff. **StemWatch + `/get_stem_tallies` live** (§§38, 47–48, 55) — per-successor stem outcomes recorded via `shekyl_relay_zone_record_stem`/`record_arrival`, snapshot through `net_node.inl:2200`; records only, thresholds deliberately open (§36.1/§37.3), so Q-10's selection round has its input signal already flowing. **§63 discovery (same round): the anonymity zone has never run a stem** — the D++ dispatch is clearnet-gated (`levin_notify.cpp:1222`); i2p/tor diffuse to their outbound set with the wire marked `dandelionpp_fluff` while the txpool is told `stem`, so the embargo arms with no stem existing. **F-12 RETRACTED** on its own instrument (sign backwards — the shipped 190 s over-provisions the anonymity path by ≥ 75 % even at 10× clearnet latency); "should the anonymity zone stem?" scoped as its own round (§64). Landing the adopted set is the next RP cut.
