The §66.3 effective-scalar policy remains open behind the owed clearnet hop-distribution measurement (§65.3) — the adopted value is its measured floor. UPDATE 2026-08-08: **§64 RULED — the anonymity zone stems** (§89, maintainer ruling): Tor is a transport like the clear internet, and changing the transport does not change the graph. That is §18 restated, so the three C++ sites still branching on transport (`levin_notify.cpp:936` initial `relay_update_stems`+`relay_wake::arm`, `:976` `new_out_connection`, `:1222` the `send_txs` dispatch gate) are the contradiction, not the fix. **The decision arrives with a price §64 had not computed**: §63.2's ≥75 % headroom was an artifact of the posture being retired (the zone fluffed at the first node, so stem length was 1 with certainty — `q=100` in the derivation). Recomputed at `q=20` (verified unchanged for the anon zone: `relay_zone_params` carries stems+epoch only), the required embargo is **exactly the shipped 190 s at clearnet-parity hop** and exceeds it everywhere above (250 s @ 500 ms, 366 s @ 1050 ms, 499 s @ 1750 ms) — and a rendezvous path cannot be at parity, so the shipped global **under-provisions** a stemming anonymity zone, the privacy-losing direction. **The embargo therefore goes per-zone, not global-at-worst-zone**: F-7's precedent does not transfer, by §63.2's own keeper (`fluff_return_ms` crosses transports because a fluff wave returns over whatever network the node is on; `time_between_hop_ms` cannot), and §59's coherence makes the per-zone quantity well-defined; §75's test picks the same answer independently (a global would give clearnet a 366 s embargo sized for a path it never touches — scalar-at-8 one axis over). Mechanism is §65.4's **two reserved bits** with `zone::invalid == 0`, already designed. Disclosure **measured, not argued** (`zone_embargo_disclosure.rs`, both controls: identical zones TV=0, a 4098 s zone accuracy 0.9108) — the closure is **vantage** (collecting self-fluffs with arming times requires a peer on that zone, who already knows the tx arrived over Tor), and the signal-strength leg is recorded **NOT load-bearing** because it is price-dependent: accuracy 0.5506 @ 500 ms but 0.6712 @ 1750 ms (five self-fluffs for 95 %), so it evaporates exactly when the measurement lands high. **Next cut: the three gates and the txpool zone field land in ONE change** (§89.5 — landing gates first ships a known privacy-losing window on purpose), carrying `(shape, zone)` on one interface change, with an interim worst-zone `hop` narrowing when the measurement arrives. **That measurement is onion-to-onion rendezvous** (the anon zone addresses peers by `.onion`, `src/net/tor_address.h`) — **no exit relay appears in the topology a stem will ever use**. Six of the 33 `levin_notify` gtests change expectation (`private_{stem,forward,local}_{without,with}_padding`, all asserting `dandelionpp_fluff == true`); `private_fluff_*` is unaffected. `hop_sensitivity::anonymity_zone_origin_is_over_provisioned_not_under` **did not fire and could not have** — it hard-codes `fluff_probability_pct = 100`, making it vacuous by input on the precise axis its comment advertises as guarded. UPDATE 2026-08-10: **§89.8 — three corrections from the #427 review round, one of them a live defect.** (1) **§30.5 was firing**: deleting the transport gate also deleted the inherited rule that a `local` send over i2p/tor keeps claiming `local`, and `local` is the class that routes the txpool backstop to `private_req`/`zone::invalid` rather than `public_req`. Since `upgrade_relay_method` is monotone, `dandelionpp_notify`'s pre-send `stem` record (and its stem-failure `fluff` record, written with **zero bytes sent**) moved a Tor-originated transaction out of `local` permanently and the next pool re-relay broadcast the user's own transaction on clearnet from the origin's own IP — §30.5's forbidden path, on the **normal** arm not an error arm. Fixed by `cryptonote::originated_stays_in_zone` (`enums.h`, beside `r1_coherence_keeps_origin`): an origin on a non-public zone keeps `local` whatever the transport did; relayed traffic still records `stem` so the per-zone embargo is drawn, clearnet origins unchanged, wire behaviour unchanged, **no persisted zone field** (§89.2's back-out reconfirmed). Witnessed by `run_private_round`'s per-round class assertion plus a one-class-only negative control. (2) **R-1's coherence branch did not wake** — §89.7 asserted it did, across the very witness gap it had just named. §63.8's chain has an unrecorded fifth link that §89 did not touch: `tx_pool.cpp:360` refuses to propagate `forward` into `tvc.m_relay`, so an anonymity arrival never reaches `relay_transactions` at all. (3) Consequently **§89.2's one-transport premise is false on the shipped path** — the `forward` entry re-emerges at `zone::public_` (`cryptonote_core.cpp:1069,1091`) and its remaining stem hops run on clearnet — and **nothing arms an anonymity-zone embargo** in the shipped configuration (`local` sets `dandelionpp_stem = 0`; relayed traffic cannot reach an anon record site). The per-zone mechanism is landed, tested and clearnet-live, and **inert on i2p/tor** until the receive path relays at arrival; that is now the FOLLOWUPS item, re-filed from "witness a live branch" to "make an anonymity arrival relay at arrival". Also in-round: the zone handle now takes a zone byte so its stem-observation window is drawn from the same per-zone parameters as the embargo (it was clearnet-only, 2.6× short, which would have reported every honest anonymity peer as withholding); the embargo tables are one per **parameter class** (`DandelionParams::adopted_class`) rather than one per zone; the disclosure instrument moved from two-million-term sums to closed forms. UPDATE 2026-08-19: **two rulings separate the axes this subsystem kept collapsing (§93), and the inherited covert branch is DELETED (§2.9 step 4).** (1) **Dandelion++ runs on every zone regardless of noise** — noise masks the node↔proxy wire against an *external* observer, D++ defends against an *internal* adversarial peer, so neither substitutes for the other (§64's sybil-substitution fallacy in operational form). The covert branch in `send_txs` collapsed carrier into phase in both directions at once (demoted `stem`→`local`, then broadcast to **every** channel); with its premise gone it is deleted rather than repaired, and nothing replaces it in C++ — the carrier attaches *below* the phase in Rust and waits for step 5's in-process caller. (2) **Noise runs only on an encrypted network** — it conceals packet *sizing*, the only thing left to read once the link is encrypted; on cleartext the observer reads the contents outright. New `LinkSecrecy` type (**birth registration, rule 94**; lives in `shekyl-relay-privacy` next to `RelayZone`, constructed only via `LinkSecrecy::of`) carries network secrecy as its own axis, refused at `Zone::new` (returns `Result<_, ZoneNewError>` so noise-on-cleartext and a mismatched channel count stay distinct) rather than silently downgraded, and derived from the zone discriminant via `RelayZone::is_encrypted` — the single site that changes if ordinary internet traffic is ever encrypted. Folded in the channel-count invariant that had been a release-compiled-out `debug_assert!`. **"Covert" retired as a term of art** (marketing, and the word two axes hid behind): `Covert*`→`Noise*`, `RelayCarrier::Covert`→`::Noise`, module `covert_queue`→`noise_queue`, and three ABI names (`SHEKYL_RELAY_CARRIER_COVERT`, `SHEKYL_RELAY_ZONE_COVERT_ENABLED`, `shekyl_relay_zone_covert_enabled`) with `shekyl_ffi.h`. C++ internals and prose naming the historical branch keep the old word deliberately. **§2.9 step 3 is satisfied by step 1, reinterpreted not skipped** — `BroadcastAllZones` *is* Rust naming the zone set, and under Design A a `fanout(configured)->configured` FFI would be an identity function (rule 21). Coverage transfer tabled at `COVER_TRAFFIC_RESTORATION.md` §1.5a; §1.5 and §43.3 bannered rather than rewritten. The replacement C++ witness `noise_does_not_override_the_phase` **passed against the restored branch in its first form** — the drive stopped on the quantity it asserts — and was re-armed to count sends, then verified red at 1-vs-2. UPDATE 2026-08-20: **Tor transit PRE-REGISTERED before the instrument runs (§94).** `ANON_ZONE_TRANSIT_ASSUMPTION_MS = 1625` is the dominant input to `F′` — §91.6 measured the admissible region at +15.2 % against transit's +42.9 %, so `beta` moves the constant by ~1/7 of what this assumption does — and it is a *labelled assumption*, not a measurement. **`ANON_ZONE_TRANSIT_MEASURED_MS` registers here at birth (rule 94)**; it will carry §87.2's `Provenance` field and the assumption constant is deleted in the same commit, not left beside its successor. §94.1 records the premise change that unblocks this: §91.6 demanded the 'complete mechanism', written when §2.9 step 4 was expected to wire the carrier — it did not, and §2.9a records step 5 as blocked. That splits the quantity: **regime 1** (ordinary encrypted transport, one onion-to-onion traversal) is complete today and measurable; **regime 2** (the noise carrier) is regime 1 plus `fragments × cadence draw`, which is **arithmetic, not a second measurement**, and is already pre-registered at `COVER_TRAFFIC_RESTORATION.md` §2.8 rather than duplicated. §94.2 freezes five degrees of freedom: onion-to-onion only (no exit relay — §89.5 forbids a clearnet-vs-exit substitute); in-band circuit rebuilds stay in the distribution but send-failure-and-re-plan does not (that is the backstop's event, §92); the modal transaction as payload, sized from a real one rather than a remembered constant; two nodes on one host with separate Tor daemons so the clock is shared and one-way latency is direct (RTT/2 is the inferior fallback, assuming a path symmetry onion rendezvous does not guarantee); p90 adopted with p50/p99 reported. **Both tails are pre-registered** — the constant's own doc anticipates only narrowing, but a measured p90 **above** 1625 raises it, because under-estimating shortens the embargo (privacy-losing) while over-estimating costs only black-hole recovery latency. Convergence: ≥5 sessions over ≥3 days spanning peak and off-peak, ≥200 samples each, stop when a further session moves the pooled p90 <5 % — Tor latency is diurnal, so 'one time of day' is this round's 'one seed', the §90 failure. Non-convergence inside 10 sessions **is** the result. Public Tor, not an owned testnet (a private harness measures the harness). **The re-derivation is the successor round with a named blocker**: `F′`/embargo/wallet-timeout/§44 pins move together and need the flood-suite reconciliation first, since five files still hold transit-less numbers beside a transit-bearing instrument. UPDATE 2026-08-20 (rig + two more pre-sample amendments): the §94 rig is built as `shekyl-tor-transit-spike` — a DISPOSABLE spike (rule 15, labelled at birth) deleted once the constant lands. It reuses `shekyl-tor`'s `ManagedTor` + `ADD_ONION` recipe but **not** `shekyl-sp-t3-spike`, despite that crate already being a Tor latency harness with a caller-supplied payload: its `timed_fetch` goes through `p-serve` and is a ROUND TRIP, which is application handling in the path plus the RTT estimator §94.5 named inferior. Two Tor daemons rather than SP-T3's one, because a single daemon makes both ends share a guard — not two distinct nodes. **§94.6** freezes the measurement HOST as a parameter: both daemons' first hops leave it, so its uplink appears twice per sample, and a datacenter VM biases transit LOW — rule 76's warning one axis over, *the uplink that was handy* rather than the CPU. Sessions run from a floor-representative consumer connection; any datacenter arm is labelled and the floor arm governs. **§94.7** records a MISSING TERM: `hop = transit + verification + scheduling` has no slot for the node's own Tor/TLS/circuit crypto (grep over the doc and `verify_cost.rs` returns nothing), which on a Pi 4 is not zero — invisible in the under-provisioning direction, the same sign as the missing transit term. The Pi is the right instrument for that fourth term and the WRONG host for transit: the board is not the variable (the uplink is), and measuring transit on a slower board would fold hardware into transit and break §86.1's acquittal exactly as §94.5 narrowed it. Explicitly NOT measured in this round; reopening criterion is that it be measured or ruled negligible with a number before the constant is composed into a shipped `hop`. Rig operations note: the round is calendar-bound, so sessions run detached (`setsid`) writing to PERSISTENT storage — three earlier attempts produced zero countable samples (a pinned-onion seed that poisoned HSDir descriptors, VSCode teardown, and a session-scoped scratchpad wiped mid-run), and only the last of those was a process-lifetime problem. UPDATE 2026-08-21 (§94.8, corrected before merge): a first draft claimed transit-dominance makes err-high a first-order privacy loss and made per-zone `F` the presumed default on privacy grounds — BOTH wrong, and §44.3 already held the refutation. §44.3 separates the wire `F` (§6.7's leak cost) from `fluff_return_ms` the derivation input (which the transit constant feeds): over-estimating the input lengthens the embargo and REDUCES prefix-fire leak (2.91→2.23%), so err-high is privacy-SAFE at a recovery-latency cost, magnitude-independently — transit-dominance does not reopen the sign. Corrected §94.8: (1) compose at the measured p90, not p90+margin, on PROVENANCE grounds (§87.2, don't contaminate a measured value with the assumption's cushion) not privacy; (2) p90 is a privacy FLOOR (below it under-provisions → more leak, §65/66) not a ceiling (above is privacy-safe, barred only by provenance); (3) per-zone `F` stays a LIVENESS call (§44.3), amplified by the transit gap but not a privacy default — if built the axis is POSTURE not zone (record_arrival fans to every zone; §18-no-adaptive forces each zone to cover its worst posture → clearnet=clearnet-flood, anon=Tor-only-flood, two determinate values). §89.2's keeper (returns-over-both, no per-zone value) is Design-B-stale, tenth instance, superseded-by-A; §44.3 governs and is not stale. **UPDATE 2026-08-28 (rule 94, cadence round #577): Q-11 Unit 2's CONSTANTS are derived and shipped; its SHAPE ruling is not.** The covert cadence is `3.333 s + U[0, 3.334 s]` (mean exactly 5 000 ms) against a per-node sustained ceiling of 16 KiB/s enforced as a `const` assert, and the denominator question `COVER_TRAFFIC_RESTORATION.md` §3.3 left owed is ruled **per node**. Re-measured at the new cadence (§56.7): bounded's residual channel falls 0.120 → 0.058 against chance 0.050, a ~7x reduction that is significant at 2.9 sd. **Still open, and the reason this row does not read CLOSED:** §56.5 ruled the family **memoryless** and the shipped law is still bounded uniform — decided, unimplemented, and now tracked as a FOLLOWUPS one-liner (pre-genesis) with §57's three exits and §58.2's threshold `theta`, both priced at the retired 12.5 s mean. |

Journal edge `Dispatched\|PresumedDead → Abandoned` + I-2 reference migration in one crash-atomic save; keys retained so OUTBOUND proofs survive; locks release on evidence (watchdog), never intent; `-29108 ABANDON_STATE_FORBIDS` for terminal rows, `-29400` for unknown txids, idempotent success, no force path; `ABANDONED` joins the `Transfer.state` vocabulary and filters. Closes the Phase 4c rescan-escape FOLLOWUP's abandon half (the wedge half closed with PR-SJ-1) (verified: `cargo test -p shekyl-wallet-rpc`; `git grep abandon_tx_persisted` → `engine/abandon_tx.rs`). UPDATE 2026-08-07 (PR-SJ-2, `feat/wallet-rpc-outgoing-sj2`): OUTGOING send history — `get_transfers` / `get_transfer_by_id` project `SendJournalBlock` rows (`project.rs::outgoing_transfer_view`, `queries.rs::collect_transfers`), closing the Phase 4b OUTGOING-filter FOLLOWUP. Every journal state gets its own wire value instead of collapsing into a neighbour: `TerminalRejected` → `FAILED`, `PresumedDead` → `DROPPED` (new enum value — the confirmed-absent watchdog has released the input locks, so `PENDING` would contradict the balance the same wallet reports). `Transfer.block_height` is now optional and means inclusion height only: a never-mined send carries none, which keeps `since_height` monotonic across a reorg and stops it hiding unsettled sends from a polling client. A malformed `get_transfer_by_id` id answers `-32602`, not a lying `-29400`. `SendRecord::sent_amount()` owns the Σ-recipients definition so the read path returns an error rather than panicking under `panic = "abort"`. CLI `transfers` gained a fee column and prints `—` for a row with no inclusion height. Annotation / `attributes` / GUI enablement remain open under SJ-DQ-7 (verified: `cargo test -p shekyl-wallet-rpc -p shekyl-engine-state -p shekyl-cli`; `git grep collect_transfers` → `shekyl-wallet-rpc/src/queries.rs`). ## 5. What is live vs. built-but-unwired vs. missing (Stage 3 slice + wallet submit-verdict path)

Verified at `dev` = `fabc2b7c0` (docs-flow repair 2026-08-28; code-anchored rows that still cite an older SHA keep that SHA in the cell):

| Layer | State | Evidence anchor |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `StakeEngine` persona/bond/scan surface | **Live** — spawned by orchestrator lifecycle for staker wallets | `engine/lifecycle.rs` (`spawn_stake_engine_if_staker`) |
| Bond vin construction (`plan_bond_post` → `BondPostPlacement`) | **Live** (inert output — the constructed vin carries no on-vin signature (SA-2b) and is not yet dispatched in production; surface-A signing rides `AssembleBond`) | `engine/stake_engine/persona.rs` |
| `P`-scan pipeline (SP-0…SP-7) | **Wired (WI-1)** — `start_pscan_if_staker` called from `shekyl-wallet-rpc`'s wallet lifecycle (staker open/close); closure proven by a behavioral test, not the `dead_code` lint | `engine/pscan/start.rs`; `shekyl-wallet-rpc/src/lifecycle.rs` |
| `P` transport (`PTorClient`, `PRpc`, `PBlockSource`) | **Built, unwired** | `shekyl-p-transport`, `engine/pscan/` |
| Persona serving host (`PersonaServingHost`, `ServeSet`, `PinnedServeSet`, `Staleness`/`StalenessBound`) — SH-1, SH-2a | **Built, unwired** — the composition is complete and tested (serve-set provenance, pin witness, endpoint/onion lifetime, ordered teardown), and nothing in the wallet constructs one yet. **SH-2a closed the refresh axis** §9.7 item 5 left open: `PersonaServingHost::refresh` re-pins the whole record-derived set unconditionally on every tick (acquiring needs no finality; releasing, still unimplemented, does), the production `ServeSetPinner` is `EngineServeSetPinner` (bond record over the persona transport + one curve-tree actor `pin_serve_set` round trip), and `Staleness`/`StalenessBound` are the tripwire for a refresh that has stopped or is failing — one clock (`ServingReader::sync_tip_height`) stamped at mint and re-read, plus a host-side count of failed attempts so a curve-tree fail-stop that freezes ingest and pinning together cannot read healthy. Fail-stop recovery rides `WriterRecovery` (`shekyl-curve-tree`), the write capability kept off the cloneable `ServingReader`. **SH-2b-1 landed** (#464): `StakeEngine` answers `PersonaOnionIdentityOf` with an expanded `OnionIdentity`, never the `hs_id_seed`. **SH-2b-2 LANDED**: `Engine::start_serving_if_staker` constructs the host behind a gate-6 §10.9 launch standoff (independent of open, so the onion does not reanimate in lockstep with principal sync), refreshes on its own cadence with the staleness bound armed only once caught up, reports five conditions through OA-1, and is parked by the embedder in an `OpenTasks` bundle beside the `PScanHandle` (shutdown is serving-first). `TorServiceConfig` is assembled under one rule — **every knob is a fingerprint**: data dir derived (`<P>.wallet.tor`), binary source discovered with one device override, supervisor policy unexposed, and no serve-or-not toggle. **What SH-2b-2 does NOT close, and every item looks done from outside** — the host starts, tests pass, alarms have projections, constants have one home, so a reader of the tree would reasonably conclude the serving path is exercised: (1) `PersonaServingHost` has **never published a descriptor** — every test drives it with fakes or a binary that cannot pass the hash gate, and the first real publish is still unlanded — W₂ was ruled rather than measured, so that publish is not owed by a W₂ rig; (2) `SERVING_MAX_STREAMS` is the carried SPIKE-PIN-1 placeholder, now named in one place a measurement can move; (3) the launch window is marked **UNDERIVED** with its reopening criterion (an open-frequency model, or a W₂ timing bound) — it is sized against open *frequency* because each open is a fresh sample and `E[min over n] = window/(n+1)`. Item (4), the `RolledBack`/`PinsDropped` projections having no producer exercised under a real `rollback_to_fork`, was **closed in the same PR**: the store-level link (rollback deletes pin rows → `members_missing_pins` reports them) lived only in a doc comment and is now walked, plus its recovery half (a re-pin clears it, which is why it is an `Episode` and not a latch) **One §9.7 hazard stays open**: the sealed decoder that would close `ServeSet` provenance against forgery as well as drift (item 3) | `shekyl-p-host`; store seam in `shekyl-curve-tree` (`ServingReader`, `WriterRecovery`, `LeafStore::pin_serve_set`); engine seam in `engine/stake_engine/serve_set_source.rs` |

| Principal stake/unstake/drain lifecycle | **Homed; the exit path is BUILT and deliberately UNREACHABLE (PR-P4, 2026-08-26).** `PRINCIPAL_STAKE_LIFECYCLE.md` (Round 1) freezes the §2 method surface and §3 firewall discipline; DQ1/DQ2/DQ5/DQ6 are closed, DQ3 (drain output-count) and DQ4 (bond-funding shape) are **re-gated to gate-6 Round 4**. **Current state.** The `Unbond` producer exists: `build_unbond_vin` for the vin and `AssembleUnbond` for the whole persona-bound exit transaction — typed `P`-space funding, payout to `P`'s own base address, and the surface-A `pqc_auths` slot under `bond_spend_pk`, the key `archival_debit_auth_pin` pins. It is not reachable: `pub(crate)`, no RPC method, no CLI verb, wallet-RPC `unstake` RESERVED. **Two** things still sit between that producer and a working verb (the walk is not one of them, and neither is the submit battery — both landed; the superseded readings are recorded at the end of this row): nothing **dispatches** the assembled bytes, and `unstake` is the composed `unbond()` = post + a *decorrelated* drain. The entry and drain legs ARE user-reachable (WI-RPC-5: `stake_in`, `drain`, `get_drain_balance`); `claim` and `get_stakes` are REJECTED, not pending (emission claims are engine-side; `principal_stakes()` is RPC-forbidden as the P↔principal edge). **The live hazard this row must carry.** `pscan/task.rs` `dispatch_retires` calls `retire_bonded_persona`, which irreversibly wipes the persona's bond-spend key, gated on a `RetirementWitness` built from pscan evidence and on the funded gate. The consumer is driven and the producer now exists, so what holds the irreversible path closed is that nothing user-facing can assemble-and-submit yet — unreachability is load-bearing rather than incidental, and any caller outside `cfg(test)` re-arms the hazard. Until 2026-08-29 native submit was a second, independent layer here — it would have refused a dispatched Unbond even if a caller reached it. It no longer would, so unreachability is the only layer left. The binding condition lives where an implementer will hit it: `api/wallet_rpc.yaml`'s RESERVED `unstake` entry. **Native submit no longer refuses an Unbond** (§8.7.1.1 landed), so unreachability is now the *whole* of what holds the path closed rather than one of two independent layers — the margin narrowed, and the next PR is the one that spends it. **Superseded — history, not present state (recorded so the change is auditable; do not read these as current).** Until 2026-08-29 it also asserted that native `/submit_transaction` refuses `Unbond` as `Malformed` — true when written, discharged by the §8.7.1.1 fact set. Until 2026-08-26 this row asserted that the Unbond post producer *did not exist* ("every `BondPostKind::Unbond` site is a detector or a test") and that *the regtest walk belongs before the producer ships*. Both were accurate when written. `build_unbond_vin` now produces the kind, and the walk ships as its own PR (ruled 2026-08-26) because a walk sharing a PR with the code it judges creates pressure to soften the walk when it goes red — a split that is safe only because unreachability, not ordering, is what protects the path. An earlier correction to this row appended those facts as an update and left the stale claims standing in the present tense; per this document's own method note, the cleared-gate sweep corrects at source, so they are rewritten here rather than contradicted below. The drain confirmation/prune driver is **half wired** (corrected 2026-08-27, PR #572, the sweep this row was owed once #564 merged and stopped owning it): a drain — and an emission claim — that CONFIRMS now releases its seal, retired against the reservation leaving the wallet's live funding set. Terminal-reject prune is not wired, so a transaction the network rejects terminally never spends its inputs, never settles, and holds its one-live gate shut; a stall alarm names it in the operator log rather than leaving it silent. The claim half was the sharper find: `remove_claim` had callers only under `cfg(test)`, so a persona that claimed once silently stopped claiming forever. **Schema moved with it: `PENDING_POST_VERSION` v7 → v8** (rule 42; snapshot + paired-bump gate green). Retiring on reservation-absence needs one live record per gindex, which the seal-time union check alone does not give across an assembly — a record that seals, confirms and retires inside another assembly's window frees the gindex, so that assembly's union check admits an input the retired record already spent, and the next tick reads its absence as *this* record's confirmation. The block therefore carries a monotonic count of reservation-releasing operations, compared at the seal against the value the assembly snapshotted; the pending block is read **before** the pscan seal (`load_seal_basis`, whose private fields make the pair unconstructable by hand) so a settlement can never pair stale funding with a current generation **UPDATE 2026-08-28 (PR #575): slice 3's walk LANDED, and it did NOT lift the reachability gate.** The split was ratified in the PR-P4 row: the three named observables are engine-level, so the walk is an **engine** walk (`engine/retire_walk.rs`, `#[cfg(test)]`, driving the real `pscan_sweep` over a synthetic `BlockSource`), and it asserts all three — the wipe and the funded gate via `persona_canonical_id` (a *different* handler over the same `held` map), and seal-then-act by crashing at the seal and restarting, bite-verified by hoisting `dispatch_retires` above the seal. **What still holds the gate: no RPC method, no CLI verb, nothing dispatching the assembled bytes, and native `/submit_transaction` refusing Unbond.** The gate wording in `api/wallet_rpc.yaml` and `WALLET_RPC_README.md` was re-anchored to those conditions in the same PR — it previously read *"RESERVED stands until that walk runs"*, which would have read as SATISFIED the moment a walk landed. **A gate tied to an event expires when the event happens; tie it to the condition.** The byte-level proposition is the separately registered **daemon walk** (this walk never encodes an `Unbond`), blocked on the Unbond submit fact set. **UPDATE 2026-08-29 (PR-A): the Unbond SUBMIT FACT SET LANDED, and it did not lift the gate either.** `DAEMON_SUBMIT_VERDICT.md` §8.7.1.1 now pins the UB rows and `shekyl-daemon-rpc`'s battery dispatches `verify_unbond_bond_post` — the same function the block path calls — so native `/submit_transaction` **accepts** a well-formed Unbond. That closes the third of the four gate conditions. **Still holding it: no RPC method, no CLI verb, nothing dispatches the assembled bytes.** The debit arm does NOT inherit BP5: the credit arm pins the slot's auth key to `P`'s identity key, which is the key a serving host holds, so UB3 pins the record's committed `bond_spend_pk` instead — that pin was lifted out of `blockchain.cpp` into `shekyl-archival-retention::debit_auth_pin` with both callers converted, because a second copy of the one check with no recovery is where a wrong edit lands. HoldingsUpdate/Rebond fact sets deliberately NOT built (rule 21: no producer). Phase D re-checks only the terminal fact, and that fact is the record's **balance**, not its presence: an exit preserves the row with a zero bonded total, so a presence-keyed debit re-check is inert. The contingent facts are pool-scope, block validation arbitrates (§8.7.2 E13 posture). Next: the engine `submit_unbond` seam + the registered daemon walk (PR-B), then reachability (PR-C) | `PRINCIPAL_STAKE_LIFECYCLE.md`; `engine/stake_engine/retire.rs`, `engine/pscan/task.rs` (`dispatch_retires`); `shekyl-daemon-rpc/src/submit/{facts,verifier,ffi_shim}.rs` |

## 8. Verified-complete archive policy

This section is the durable home of the **verified-complete archive policy**
previously named only in commit messages (`29d267de8`, `675075610`,
`f9f7085df`). Completeness ≠ archival. Living contracts of record stay in
`docs/design/` forever even when implementation is done.

### What belongs where

| Keep in `docs/design/` | Move to `docs/completed/` |
| --- | --- |
| Living specs / contracts of record (e.g. `ARCHIVAL_CONSENSUS_STATE`, `REWARD_EMISSION_LEG`, `DAEMON_SUBMIT_VERDICT`, wire formats) | Finished plan / closeout / preflight / pin / retired-concept record |
| Active umbrellas with open phases (`WALLET_REWRITE_PLAN`, Gate-6, …) | Track whose *owned* work is closed; residues live elsewhere (FOLLOWUPS / sibling SoT) |
| Mixed docs (“landed” + named open residue owned by *this* doc) | Superseded-do-not-implement historical records (after link repair) |

### Eligibility (all three required)

1. **Self-check.** Status banner ∈ {RETIRED, CLOSED-as-record, Round CLOSED
   with no owned open work}; no “DRAFT / in review / pending phase” that
   *this* doc still owns.
2. **Index agreement.** A §2/§4/§5 row (if any) agrees, or the doc is a pure
   closeout sibling of an already-archived track.
3. **Code verify on `dev`.** For landed claims, `git grep` the symbols/PRs;
   for retired claims, confirm absence (or tombstone) as the doc asserts.
   Doc-prose alone is insufficient (rule 94).

### Non-goals

- Do **not** archive by “implementation landed” alone — that is the living-SoT
  hold rule.
- Do **not** rewrite historical CHANGELOG narrative that says a doc “was under
  `docs/design/`” unless the CHANGELOG entry is a clickable relative link that
  would 404 after the move.
- Do **not** bulk-move an entire directory in one PR; batch by risk class
  (retired → closed audits → Stage 0/1 siblings).

### Move checklist

1. Confirm eligibility triad above for each file.
2. `git mv docs/design/X.md docs/completed/X.md`.
3. Rewrite inbound references: `rg -l 'X\.md'` under `docs/`, `.cursor/rules/`,
   and any CI/script path citations that use a `docs/design/` prefix. Prefer
   path-correct markdown links; basename-only code comments
   (`ARCHIVAL_FOO.md §N`) may stay (the M5 citation gate resolves by basename
   under `docs/`).
4. Fix relative links *inside* the moved file (`../design/` for living SoTs;
   sibling completed docs stay same-directory; `../../.cursor/rules/` depth is
   unchanged).
5. Update this index (§2/§4/§5/§7 rows that listed the file under `design/`).
6. Spot-check: no remaining clickable `docs/design/X.md` (or
   `](X.md)` from a file that assumed co-location in `design/`) for moved `X`.

### Candidate ledger (as of 2026-07-26)

**Batch 1 — moved this sweep (verified-complete):**

| Doc | Why archived |
| --- | --- |
| `docs/completed/ARCHIVAL_REWARD_GATE_M1.md` | RETIRED 2026-07-19 (#346); production machinery removed; historical record |
| `docs/completed/ARCHIVAL_COVER_DRAW.md` | RETIRED 2026-07-21; count-dependent `span(C)` deleted-not-built |
| `docs/completed/REWARD_EMISSION_E3_GATING_ROUND.md` | Round CLOSED 2026-07-07; SoT remains `REWARD_EMISSION_LEG` / `VIN_PLAN` |
| `docs/completed/ARCHIVAL_EXIT_STANDOFF_FD4_WINDOW.md` | Closed archival record; mechanism deletion landed; X-1…X-3 CLOSED in §2 |
| `docs/completed/CONFIDENTIAL_STAKING.md` | **Deleted 2026-08-26** (claim-era). Stub pattern withdrawn — git history is the archive. Do not recreate. |

**Batch 2 — moved 2026-08-26:** Stage 0 + Stage 1 PR 4–7
(`docs/completed/STAGE_0_HARNESS.md`,
`docs/completed/STAGE_1_PR_{4,5,6,7}_*.md`). CLOSED-as-record banners
applied. Living SoT remains `V3_ENGINE_TRAIT_BOUNDARIES.md`.

**Batch 3 — moved 2026-08-26:** `ARCHIVAL_SERVE_CREDIT_EQUIVALENCE_AUDIT`,
`ARCHIVAL_TM1_CLUSTERING`, `CT1_ROUND1_PINS` → `docs/completed/`.

**Hold (do not move):** living SoTs and mixed fronts — e.g.
`CURVE_TREE_CLIENT`, `DAEMON_SUBMIT_VERDICT`, `EMISSION_CLAIM_BUILDER`,
`ARCHIVAL_BOND_WI4_MEASUREMENT`, `ARCHIVAL_BOND_*` / Gate-6 / construction
docs.

**Superseded-with-stub pattern: WITHDRAWN for claim-era staking specs.**
The one instance (`CONFIDENTIAL_STAKING.md` stub pointing at STRATUM) is
the cautionary example. Claim-era documents are **deleted**, not stubbed.
Git history is the archive. Do not recreate `docs/design/CONFIDENTIAL_STAKING.md`.
Archival staking SoTs stay in `design/` (`V3_STAKER_ARCHIVAL.md`,
`PHASE_2B_FSM_RETOOL.md`, `PRINCIPAL_STAKE_LIFECYCLE.md`, `REWARD_EMISSION_*`,
`ARCHIVAL_*`).

For other retired designs that are easy to mistake for current SoT, prefer
deletion (or a CLOSED-as-record file in `docs/completed/` only) over a
`design/` stub.

## Relay layer → C++ dependency inventory (p2p migration cost)

**Measured 2026-08-02 from the §55 work, recorded here rather than in the
relay doc because this is where someone costing the p2p migration would
look.** The relay layer is Rust-owned except for exactly two dependencies,
and **both are containment, not entanglement** — which is what makes the
migration schedulable rather than something to negotiate mid-flight.

| # | dependency | where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **Zone handle lifetime** — `RelayZoneHandle` is created and owned per network zone by C++ | [`levin_notify.cpp`](../../src/cryptonote_protocol/levin_notify.cpp) `make_relay_zone`, held in `detail::zone::relay` |
| 2 | **Connection table** — the `ConnectionId` → peer/address join, and zone membership | [`net_node.inl`](../../src/p2p/net_node.inl) `m_network_zones`, `for_each_connection` |

**Decay warning: this is a claim about code state and it ages at the code's
rate, not the estimate's.** The visible symptom is `/get_stem_tallies`'
round trip — Rust relay → FFI → C++ → FFI → Rust RPC — which exists only to
cross (1), and which will recur for every relay-facing readout added before
the migration. If a third dependency appears, add it here; if the list is
still two when p2p is scheduled, the estimate still holds.
